# Daily Summary: 2025-08-20

### Matrix-Game 2.0: An Open-Source, Real-Time, and Streaming Interactive World Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13009v1)
- **Authors**: Xianglong He, Chunli Peng, Zexiang Liu, Boyang Wang, Yifan Zhang, Qi Cui, Fei Kang, Biao Jiang, Mengyin An, Yangyang Ren, Baixin Xu, Hao-Xiang Guo, Kaixiong Gong, Cyrus Wu, Wei Li, Xuchen Song, Yang Liu, Eric Li, Yahui Zhou
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in interactive video generations have demonstrated diffusion model's potential as world models by capturing complex physical dynamics and interactive behaviors. However, existing interactive world models depend on bidirectional attention and lengthy inference steps, severely limiting real-time performance. Consequently, they are hard to simulate real-world dynamics, where outcomes must update instantaneously based on historical context and current actions. To address this, we present Matrix-Game 2.0, an interactive world model generates long videos on-the-fly via few-step auto-regressive diffusion. Our framework consists of three key components: (1) A scalable data production pipeline for Unreal Engine and GTA5 environments to effectively produce massive amounts (about 1200 hours) of video data with diverse interaction annotations; (2) An action injection module that enables frame-level mouse and keyboard inputs as interactive conditions; (3) A few-step distillation based on the casual architecture for real-time and streaming video generation. Matrix Game 2.0 can generate high-quality minute-level videos across diverse scenes at an ultra-fast speed of 25 FPS. We open-source our model weights and codebase to advance research in interactive world modeling.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the Matrix-Game 2.0 paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents Matrix-Game 2.0, an open-source interactive world model designed for real-time streaming and generation of long videos. It addresses the limitations of existing interactive video generation models, specifically their inability to achieve real-time performance and their struggles with long-term consistency. The authors overcome these challenges through (1) a large-scale data production pipeline for Unreal Engine and GTA5 environments, (2) an action injection module allowing for frame-level mouse and keyboard control, and (3) a few-step distillation-based causal architecture optimized for speed.  The system reportedly achieves 25 FPS generation speeds while maintaining minute-long temporal consistency and precise action controllability. The authors release the model weights and codebase to foster further research in interactive world modeling.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   The combination of a large-scale, diverse dataset creation pipeline *specifically designed* for interactive video generation, coupled with a distilled causal architecture for speed, demonstrates strong novelty. The authors highlight the limitations of existing datasets, justifying the need for their custom pipeline with improved annotations and alignment of actions with visual data.
    *   The decision to move away from text-based guidance, focusing instead on learning directly from visual data and actions to develop spatial intelligence, is a notable conceptual novelty.  Most current research leans heavily on text conditioning.
    *   The application of Self-Forcing with specific modifications (chunking, attention localization) for auto-regressive distillation is a worthwhile engineering contribution, optimized for real-time performance.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Impact on Real-time Interaction:** The reported 25 FPS generation speed is a significant practical achievement, positioning Matrix-Game 2.0 as a truly *interactive* world model rather than simply a video generation system. This unlocks potential applications in gaming, virtual environments, and robotics.
    *   **Focus on Visual Intelligence:** The emphasis on visual understanding over textual guidance is potentially significant. If successful, it may lead to more robust and physically grounded world models, less reliant on potentially noisy or incomplete textual descriptions.
    *   **Open-Source Contribution:** Releasing the model weights and codebase greatly increases the potential impact of the work, enabling other researchers to build upon and improve the system. This addresses a significant bottleneck in the field, making it easier to explore interactive world models.
    *   **Limitations Acknowledged:** The authors acknowledge limitations such as the limited output resolution, which is lower than typical state-of-the-art video generation, and the challenges with out-of-domain scenarios. This honest assessment strengthens the paper.

*   **Areas for Improvement:**

    *   **Stronger Evaluation:** While the GameWorld Score Benchmark is used, the paper could benefit from more rigorous quantitative evaluation across different datasets and a more thorough comparison to existing *real-time* interactive world models (if available).  Ablation studies are useful but focused on architecture choices.
    *   **Scalability and Generalization:** The evaluation focuses on Minecraft and wild scenes with a brief demonstration in GTA. Further evaluation on a broader range of environments is needed to assess its truly "universal" potential.
    *   **Detailed Architecture Explanation:** While an overview is provided, a deeper dive into specific architectural choices and hyperparameter tuning would be valuable.

**Overall:**

Matrix-Game 2.0 marks a significant step forward in real-time interactive world modeling. The focus on visual intelligence and the release of the codebase contribute significantly to the field. While the evaluation could be strengthened, the reported performance and design choices are compelling. The combination of data pipeline, distilled causal architecture, and achieved frame rate position it as a valuable contribution.
Score: 7
Rationale: The paper presents a novel framework for interactive video generation that achieves real-time performance. While it has some limitations in terms of evaluation and generalizability, its focus on visual intelligence and the release of the codebase make it a significant contribution to the field. The novelty of the combined approach (data pipeline, causal distilled architecture, visual intelligence) and the potential impact on real-time applications justifies a strong score. A score higher than 7 requires significantly better evaluations and more thorough comparisons against existing solutions.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### PC-Sampler: Position-Aware Calibration of Decoding Bias in Masked Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13021v2)
- **Authors**: Pengcheng Huang, Shuhao Liu, Zhenghao Liu, Yukun Yan, Shuo Wang, Zulong Chen, Tong Xiao
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in masked diffusion models (MDMs) have established them as powerful non-autoregressive alternatives for sequence generation. Nevertheless, our preliminary experiments reveal that the generation quality of MDMs is still highly sensitive to the choice of decoding strategy. In particular, widely adopted uncertainty-based samplers suffer from two key limitations: a lack of global trajectory control and a pronounced bias toward trivial tokens in the early stages of decoding. These shortcomings restrict the full potential of MDMs. In this work, we introduce Position-Aware Confidence-Calibrated Sampling (PC-Sampler), a novel decoding strategy that unifies global trajectory planning with content-aware informativeness maximization. PC-Sampler incorporates a position-aware weighting mechanism to regulate the decoding path and a calibrated confidence score to suppress the premature selection of trivial tokens. Extensive experiments on three advanced MDMs across seven challenging benchmarks-including logical reasoning and planning tasks-demonstrate that PC-Sampler consistently outperforms existing MDM decoding strategies by more than 10% on average, significantly narrowing the performance gap with state-of-the-art autoregressive models. All codes are available at https://github.com/NEUIR/PC-Sampler.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "PC-Sampler: Position-Aware Calibration of Decoding Bias in Masked Diffusion Models" addresses limitations in decoding strategies for masked diffusion models (MDMs).  It identifies two key problems with existing uncertainty-based sampling methods: a lack of global trajectory control and a bias towards trivial tokens early in the decoding process.  To overcome these, the authors propose PC-Sampler, a novel decoding strategy that combines a position-aware weighting mechanism to guide the decoding path with a calibrated confidence score to suppress the premature selection of trivial tokens.  Extensive experiments across various tasks demonstrate the effectiveness of PC-Sampler, showing significant improvements over existing MDM decoding strategies and narrowing the performance gap with autoregressive models. The paper demonstrates PC-Sampler's capability to improve both performance and integration with other optimization techniques making the approach more practically effective.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's core contribution, PC-Sampler, is relatively novel. While uncertainty-based sampling and control of text generation order have been explored before, the specific combination of a position-aware prior and frequency-calibrated confidence score within the MDM framework appears to be a significant improvement.  The position aware decay is novel in addressing decoding trajectories.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses an important issue in MDMs – the sensitivity to decoding strategy.  By proposing a more robust and adaptable sampling method, the work has the potential to make MDMs more practically useful and competitive with autoregressive models, especially in tasks requiring non-sequential reasoning. The experimental results are compelling, showing substantial gains across a diverse set of tasks, which demonstrates the generalizability of the proposed method.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing decoding strategies and motivates the need for a new approach.
    *   **Well-Designed Method:** PC-Sampler is thoughtfully designed, combining two complementary components to address the identified issues. The motivation for each part of PC-Sampler is explained well.
    *   **Extensive Experiments:** The authors perform extensive experiments across multiple datasets and MDM architectures, providing strong evidence for the effectiveness of their method. The ablation studies give a good understanding of each part's benefits. The experiments with efficient samplers show an important characteristic of being readily integrated.
    *   **Strong Results:**  The results convincingly demonstrate that PC-Sampler outperforms existing decoding strategies, narrowing the gap with autoregressive models.
    *   **Case Studies:** The inclusion of case studies helps to illustrate the practical benefits of PC-Sampler and provide insights into why it works.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** While the paper provides empirical evidence of PC-Sampler's effectiveness, a more rigorous theoretical analysis of its properties would be beneficial. It could have explained a bit more about how the trajectory selection works or related trajectory selection and type of task more clearly in the study.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** While the paper analyzes the impact of key hyperparameters, a more detailed discussion of how to choose optimal hyperparameter values for different tasks would be helpful.
    *   **Runtime Trade-offs:** The paper briefly mentions integrating PC-Sampler with efficient decoding techniques. A more detailed analysis of the runtime overhead of PC-Sampler compared to other decoding strategies would be beneficial.

*   **Overall Impact:** The paper is likely to have a significant impact on the field of MDMs.  It offers a practical and effective solution to a critical problem, potentially paving the way for broader adoption of MDMs in various applications.

*   **Rigor:** The paper follows standard research practices. The approach is sound and the experiments are extensive. The paper is well-written and easy to understand.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper's central idea is relatively incremental (combining existing concepts in a novel way), it tackles an important problem with demonstrable success. The broad experimental validation adds weight to the work and gives confidence in its claims. Its impact is on MDMs and does not revolutionize deep learning as a whole. A score of 7 reflects the paper's novelty, well-defined problem, strong empirical support, and likely impact on the field of MDMs, balanced against the limited theoretical analysis and room for improvement in hyperparameter tuning guidance.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### G$^2$RPO-A: Guided Group Relative Policy Optimization with Adaptive Guidance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13023v1)
- **Authors**: Yongxin Guo, Wenbo Deng, Zhenglin Cheng, Xiaoying Tang
- **Abstract**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) has markedly enhanced the reasoning abilities of large language models (LLMs). Its success, however, largely depends on strong base models with rich world knowledge, yielding only modest improvements for small-size language models (SLMs). To address this limitation, we investigate Guided GRPO, which injects ground-truth reasoning steps into roll-out trajectories to compensate for SLMs' inherent weaknesses. Through a comprehensive study of various guidance configurations, we find that naively adding guidance delivers limited gains. These insights motivate G$^2$RPO-A, an adaptive algorithm that automatically adjusts guidance strength in response to the model's evolving training dynamics. Experiments on mathematical reasoning and code-generation benchmarks confirm that G$^2$RPO-A substantially outperforms vanilla GRPO. Our code and models are available at https://github.com/T-Lab-CUHKSZ/G2RPO-A.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "G2RPO-A: Guided Group Relative Policy Optimization with Adaptive Guidance":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of improving the reasoning abilities of small language models (SLMs) using Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR), specifically the Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) algorithm.  GRPO's effectiveness is limited by the capacity of SLMs to generate high-quality reasoning steps during rollouts. To overcome this, the authors introduce "Guided GRPO" which injects ground-truth reasoning steps (guidance) into the rollout trajectories.  However, naive, fixed-length guidance doesn't significantly improve performance.  Therefore, they propose "G2RPO-A," an adaptive algorithm that dynamically adjusts the guidance length based on the model's learning progress. The paper systematically analyzes different guidance configurations, including guidance ratio and length, and demonstrates through experiments on math reasoning and code generation that G2RPO-A outperforms vanilla GRPO and simple guided baselines. The adaptive mechanism is combined with curriculum learning techniques to present increasingly difficult samples to the model, further improving training efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using guidance to improve SLM performance within the RLVR framework has some novelty, but this area is becoming rapidly populated. Prior work has explored knowledge distillation, multi-stage pre-training, and sample filtering to address the shortcomings of GRPO for SLMs. The specific contribution of dynamically adjusting the *length* of the guidance based on model performance, coupled with curriculum learning, is a valuable increment. However, the experiments lack comparison with other forms of guidance, such as *contextualized* or *distilled* guidance. The paper could also benefit from a more detailed discussion of the trade-offs between exploration and exploitation when using guided rollouts. The novelty is incrementally significant but not breakthrough.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to make RLVR more effective for SLMs, potentially democratizing access to advanced reasoning capabilities. GRPO's reliance on strong base models limits its applicability in resource-constrained settings. G2RPO-A addresses this limitation, enabling researchers and practitioners with limited computational resources to fine-tune SLMs for reasoning tasks. However, the gains achieved by G2RPO-A, while substantial relative to vanilla GRPO, might not be dramatic compared to the performance achievable with larger models, which is a relevant benchmark to consider. The thorough experimental validation and the provision of code and models enhance the paper's impact and allow for reproducibility. The adaptive guidance strategy presents a useful technique that other researchers can build on.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Systematic exploration of guidance configurations: The paper's thorough investigation of different guidance ratios and lengths is a major strength. The discovery that optimal guidance parameters depend on the task, model size, and training stage provides valuable insights for future research.
    *   Adaptive guidance mechanism: The G2RPO-A algorithm's ability to dynamically adjust guidance length based on model performance is a clever approach to balance exploration and exploitation.
    *   Curriculum learning integration:  Combining adaptive guidance with curriculum learning further improves training efficiency and overall performance.
    *   Comprehensive experimental validation: The paper provides extensive experimental results on various benchmarks, demonstrating the effectiveness of G2RPO-A across different tasks and model sizes.
    * Reproducibility: The availability of the code allows for validation and follow-up work by other researchers.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Incremental Nature:  The paper builds upon existing work on GRPO and RLVR and is an incremental improvement of these existing methods. The gains relative to existing methods outside the GRPO/RLVR space are not convincingly established.
    *   Limited Novelty in the Overall Concept: Injecting some form of "corrective" signal into RL for language tasks is a common theme in the literature. The novelty is specific to the adaptive adjustment of the guidance *length*.
    * Lack of comparison to other Guidance Approaches: Comparison to alternatives, such as contextual guidance, distilled guidance, or other state-of-the-art RLVR methods would add more weight to the claims.
    * Potential Hyperparameter Sensitivity:  While the paper demonstrates the effectiveness of G2RPO-A, the algorithm likely introduces new hyperparameters. The paper doesn't fully explore the sensitivity of G2RPO-A's performance to these hyperparameters, which might limit its practical applicability.
    * Limited Analysis on the Failure cases for G2RPO-A.

**Overall:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of RLVR for language models, particularly in the context of enhancing the reasoning abilities of small models.  The adaptive guidance mechanism and the systematic exploration of guidance configurations are the major strengths. The incremental nature of the innovation, along with the lack of a comparison with other guidance approaches, are the major weaknesses.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a solid, incrementally novel, and well-executed approach to improving RLVR for SLMs.  The systematic experimental analysis and the adaptive guidance mechanism are the key strengths. However, the contribution is not breakthrough, and a more comprehensive comparison to other relevant methods, especially related to guidance, and a deeper dive into hyperparameter sensitivity, would significantly strengthen the paper.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### The Application of Transformer-Based Models for Predicting Consequences of Cyber Attacks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13030v1)
- **Authors**: Bipin Chhetri, Akbar Siami Namin
- **Abstract**: Cyberattacks are increasing, and securing against such threats is costing industries billions of dollars annually. Threat Modeling, that is, comprehending the consequences of these attacks, can provide critical support to cybersecurity professionals, enabling them to take timely action and allocate resources that could be used elsewhere. Cybersecurity is heavily dependent on threat modeling, as it assists security experts in assessing and mitigating risks related to identifying vulnerabilities and threats. Recently, there has been a pressing need for automated methods to assess attack descriptions and forecast the future consequences of the increasing complexity of cyberattacks. This study examines how Natural Language Processing (NLP) and deep learning can be applied to analyze the potential impact of cyberattacks by leveraging textual descriptions from the MITRE Common Weakness Enumeration (CWE) database. We emphasize classifying attack consequences into five principal categories: Availability, Access Control, Confidentiality, Integrity, and Other. This paper investigates the use of Bidirectional Encoder Representations from Transformers (BERT) in combination with Hierarchical Attention Networks (HANs) for Multi-label classification, evaluating their performance in comparison with conventional CNN and LSTM-based models. Experimental findings show that BERT achieves an overall accuracy of $0.972$, far higher than conventional deep learning models in multi-label classification. HAN outperforms baseline forms of CNN and LSTM-based models on specific cybersecurity labels. However, BERT consistently achieves better precision and recall, making it more suitable for predicting the consequences of a cyberattack.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper investigates the application of transformer-based models, specifically BERT and Hierarchical Attention Networks (HAN), for predicting the consequences of cyberattacks based on textual descriptions from the MITRE CWE database. The study focuses on classifying attack consequences into five categories: Availability, Access Control, Confidentiality, Integrity, and Other. The paper compares the performance of BERT and HAN against traditional CNN and LSTM-based models. The experimental results indicate that BERT significantly outperforms the other models, achieving a high overall accuracy. While HAN shows promise in capturing document-level semantics and performs well for specific labels (Access Control and Integrity), it doesn't match BERT's overall performance. The paper argues that transformer-based models offer a scalable and practical alternative to knowledge graph-based methods for predicting cyberattack consequences.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in applying transformer-based models (BERT and HAN) to the specific problem of predicting cyberattack consequences from CWE descriptions. While BERT and HAN are established models in NLP, their application to cybersecurity consequence prediction, especially with a comparison to simpler architectures like CNNs and LSTMs, constitutes a valuable contribution. The hybrid approach combining deep architectures with attention mechanisms is another positive. The paper builds upon previous research that used CNN and LSTM approaches but expands on the idea by adding in attention mechanisms and exploring transformer-based models. The use of HAN is also a positive addition, particularly given that the literature reviewed shows that it hasn't been applied in cybersecurity contexts as extensively as BERT.

* **Significance:** Predicting cyberattack consequences is a crucial aspect of threat modeling and cybersecurity risk management. By demonstrating the superior performance of transformer-based models, the paper offers a potentially significant improvement in the accuracy and efficiency of this process. The improved accuracy could lead to more effective resource allocation and timely mitigation strategies. The scalability of the approach, compared to knowledge graphs, makes it potentially more practical for real-world applications.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the problem and its importance.
    * **Thorough Experimental Evaluation:**  The paper includes a comprehensive experimental setup comparing multiple models with relevant metrics. The comparison to the earlier CNN-LSTM based work is particularly valuable.
    * **Detailed Analysis of Results:** The paper provides a detailed analysis of the results, including label-wise performance and confusion matrices.
    * **Scalability argument:** The point that the methods are scalable is a very important point for practical application.
    * **Clear presentation and writing style:** Easy to follow with well-structured logic.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Dataset Size:** The size of the dataset (895 rows) might be a limitation, particularly for training deep learning models like BERT. Although transfer learning mitigates this, a larger dataset would strengthen the findings.
    * **Limited Ablation Studies:** While the paper compares different architectures, it could benefit from more ablation studies to understand the contribution of specific components within BERT and HAN. For instance, analyzing the effect of different attention mechanisms or layer configurations.  It needs more investigation into what drives HAN's superior performance on the specific categories.
    * **Limited Exploration of Other Transformer Models:**  The paper focuses mainly on BERT and HAN. Exploring other transformer-based models (e.g., RoBERTa, ALBERT, DistilBERT) could provide further insights and potentially even better performance. The future work suggests this.
    * **Limited contextualization of the results:** While showing that this method outperforms baseline methods, it would have been great to better contextualize the implications of this result. For example, by arguing why the models are not 'good enough'. What would be required for a cybersecurity system to use the model? What are the next steps in order to increase accuracy?
    * **Lack of Error Analysis:** Including more error analysis (e.g., identifying patterns in the misclassified examples) could provide valuable insights into the limitations of the models and guide future improvements.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by encouraging the adoption of transformer-based models for cybersecurity consequence prediction. The demonstrated performance gains could lead to practical improvements in threat modeling and risk management. It can also encourage future research exploring different transformer-based architectures, hybrid approaches, and improved data handling techniques.

**Score:** 7/10

**Rationale:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by applying transformer-based models to the important problem of predicting cyberattack consequences. The experimental results clearly demonstrate the superiority of BERT over traditional models, and the exploration of HAN offers interesting insights. The scalability of the approach is a significant advantage.

However, the paper is not without its limitations. The relatively small dataset size, the limited ablation studies, and the lack of exploration of other transformer-based models somewhat diminish its overall impact.  Therefore, while the paper offers a solid contribution, the points raised prevent a higher score. While the authors show promise, further research is needed to reach a score above a 7.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Can Large Models Teach Student Models to Solve Mathematical Problems Like Human Beings? A Reasoning Distillation Method via Multi-LoRA Interaction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13037v1)
- **Authors**: Xinhe Li, Jiajun Liu, Peng Wang
- **Abstract**: Recent studies have demonstrated that Large Language Models (LLMs) have strong mathematical reasoning abilities but rely on hundreds of billions of parameters. To tackle the challenge of poor reasoning in Small Language Models (SLMs), existing methods typically leverage LLMs to generate massive amounts of data for cramming training. In psychology, they are akin to System 1 thinking, which resolves reasoning problems rapidly based on experience and intuition. However, human learning also requires System 2 thinking, where knowledge is first acquired and then reinforced through practice. Inspired by such two distinct modes of thinking, we propose a novel method based on the multi-LoRA Interaction for mathematical reasoning Distillation (LoRID). First, we input the question and reasoning of each sample into an LLM to create knowledge-enhanced datasets. Subsequently, we train a LoRA block on the student model as an Intuitive Reasoner (IR), which directly generates Chain-of-Thoughts for problem-solving. Then, to imitate System 2 thinking, we train the Knowledge Generator (KG) and Deep Reasoner (DR), respectively. The former outputs only knowledge after receiving problems, while the latter uses that knowledge to perform reasoning. Finally, to address the randomness in the generation of IR and DR, we evaluate whether their outputs are consistent, and the inference process needs to be iterated if not. This step can enhance the mathematical reasoning ability of SLMs through mutual feedback. Experimental results show that LoRID achieves state-of-the-art performance, especially on the GSM8K dataset, where it outperforms the second-best method by 2.3%, 16.1%, 2.4%, 12.3%, and 1.8% accuracy across the five base models, respectively.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LoRID (Reasoning Distillation via Multi-LoRA Interaction), a novel method for distilling mathematical reasoning abilities from large language models (LLMs) to smaller language models (SLMs).  LoRID draws inspiration from the human learning process, specifically System 1 (intuitive) and System 2 (deliberative) thinking.  The method involves: 1) Knowledge Augmentation, where an LLM generates knowledge nuggets related to the problem; 2) Training an Intuitive Reasoner (IR) LoRA to directly generate reasoning steps; 3) Training a Knowledge Generator (KG) to output relevant knowledge and a Deep Reasoner (DR) that uses this knowledge for reasoning; and 4) A Multi-LoRA Interaction stage where IR and DR iteratively interact, providing feedback to each other until consistent outputs are achieved.  The authors demonstrate state-of-the-art performance, especially on the GSM8K dataset, and show that LoRID can be effectively integrated with existing CoT distillation methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its human-inspired approach to distilling reasoning abilities.  While data augmentation and knowledge distillation are established techniques, LoRID's explicit modeling of System 1 and System 2 thinking, the multi-LoRA interaction framework, and the knowledge generation component represent a significant departure from typical LLM-to-SLM distillation strategies.  The idea of explicitly generating knowledge and then using that knowledge in a separate reasoning module is a valuable contribution. The plug-and-play characteristic due to LoRA's application adds another element of novelty.

* **Significance:** The work addresses the crucial challenge of enabling SLMs to perform complex reasoning tasks without relying on massive computational resources.  Improving the reasoning abilities of SLMs is important for deploying AI solutions in resource-constrained environments. The experimental results, particularly the substantial gains on GSM8K, suggest that LoRID is a promising approach. Its demonstrated compatibility with existing methods significantly enhances its practicality and potential impact.  The insights into the interplay of intuitive and deliberative reasoning are also valuable and may inform future research in this area.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Human-inspired Approach:** Drawing inspiration from cognitive science is a strong point, providing a solid theoretical foundation for the method.
    * **Modular Design:** The separation of knowledge generation and reasoning allows for more targeted training and interaction.
    * **Multi-LoRA Interaction:** The iterative feedback mechanism enhances the reasoning process and handles the randomness inherent in LLM generation.
    * **Strong Experimental Results:** The state-of-the-art performance on GSM8K, along with consistent improvements across different base models, provides strong empirical evidence for the effectiveness of LoRID. The analysis of LoRID with self-consistent CoT shows that incorporating the multi-LoRA block results in better performance, also improving the efficiency.
    * **Integration with Existing Methods:** Demonstrating that LoRID can be combined with existing CoT distillation methods makes it a versatile and valuable tool.
    * **Scaling Law Discussion:** Analysis of LoRID's scaling behavior and difficulty awareness demonstrates the applicability in resource-constrained settings.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on Closed-Source LLMs for Knowledge Generation:**  The initial knowledge augmentation step depends on prompting GPT-4. The closed-source dependency makes it difficult to reproduce the exact knowledge generation step and raises concerns about the long-term availability and cost. While the distilled model is open source, the generation of training data has this external dependency.
    * **MATH Dataset Performance:** While LoRID performs well on GSM8K, its performance on the more challenging MATH dataset is not as compelling as some tool-based methods.  The ablation study also points out an improvement in all aspects, but further analysis can be made to better understand it.
    * **Computational Cost:** While LoRA is parameter efficient, the iterative inference process of LoRID could add overhead compared to simpler distillation methods.  A more detailed analysis of the inference time would be beneficial.

* **Potential Influence:** LoRID has the potential to influence the field of knowledge distillation and mathematical reasoning in SLMs. The system 1 and 2 thinking idea might trigger more work towards more human-align learning and teaching pipelines to student models. Its modular design provides a blueprint for incorporating different reasoning components and interaction mechanisms. The effectiveness of iterative refinement could also be explored in other reasoning tasks.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

LoRID presents a compelling and novel approach to distilling mathematical reasoning abilities. The human-inspired design, modular architecture, multi-LoRA interaction framework, and strong experimental results on GSM8K warrant a high score. While the reliance on a closed-source LLM for initial knowledge generation and the MATH dataset results are not as compelling, the strengths of the paper significantly outweigh the weaknesses. The potential impact of LoRID on the development of reasoning-capable SLMs is considerable. Further research focusing on reducing reliance on closed-source models and improving performance on more complex datasets would strengthen the work even further.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Büyük Dil Modelleri için TR-MMLU Benchmarkı: Performans Değerlendirmesi, Zorluklar ve İyileştirme Fırsatları
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13044v1)
- **Authors**: M. Ali Bayram, Ali Arda Fincan, Ahmet Semih Gümüş, Banu Diri, Savaş Yıldırım, Öner Aytaş
- **Abstract**: Language models have made significant advancements in understanding and generating human language, achieving remarkable success in various applications. However, evaluating these models remains a challenge, particularly for resource-limited languages like Turkish. To address this issue, we introduce the Turkish MMLU (TR-MMLU) benchmark, a comprehensive evaluation framework designed to assess the linguistic and conceptual capabilities of large language models (LLMs) in Turkish. TR-MMLU is based on a meticulously curated dataset comprising 6,200 multiple-choice questions across 62 sections within the Turkish education system. This benchmark provides a standard framework for Turkish NLP research, enabling detailed analyses of LLMs' capabilities in processing Turkish text. In this study, we evaluated state-of-the-art LLMs on TR-MMLU, highlighting areas for improvement in model design. TR-MMLU sets a new standard for advancing Turkish NLP research and inspiring future innovations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TR-MMLU, a new benchmark for evaluating large language models (LLMs) in Turkish.  TR-MMLU consists of 6,200 multiple-choice questions across 62 categories derived from the Turkish education system.  The authors evaluated several state-of-the-art LLMs using TR-MMLU and identified areas for improvement in model design for Turkish language processing. The paper highlights the limitations of existing multilingual benchmarks for Turkish and argues that TR-MMLU provides a more accurate and culturally relevant evaluation framework.  The results are published on Hugging Face.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The main novelty lies in the creation and release of the TR-MMLU benchmark itself. While individual datasets and benchmarks exist for Turkish NLP tasks (as acknowledged in the "Related Work" section), a comprehensive, education-based, multiple-choice benchmark covering a wide range of subjects appears to be a genuinely new contribution. The paper clearly articulates the shortcomings of relying on translated benchmarks and the need for a culturally and linguistically appropriate evaluation tool.  The focus on an "open" benchmark published on Hugging Face is also a practical move. However, the evaluation methodology using "prompt engineering" and reporting basic accuracy metrics is not particularly novel. The core evaluation techniques are standard practices in the field.

**Significance:** The significance stems from addressing a clear gap in Turkish NLP research. The lack of a robust, standardized benchmark has hindered the development and comparison of Turkish language models. By releasing TR-MMLU, the authors provide a much-needed resource that can facilitate progress in the field.  The paper effectively argues for the importance of evaluating models specifically on Turkish and highlights the unique challenges posed by the language's morphological complexity. However, the impact ultimately depends on the adoption of the benchmark by other researchers and developers.  The paper's significance could be increased if it had delved deeper into the types of errors made by different models, offering concrete insights into how they could be improved. A more thorough error analysis would have significantly enhanced the paper's impact.

**Strengths:**

*   **Creation of a valuable resource:** The TR-MMLU benchmark is a significant contribution to the Turkish NLP community.
*   **Clear articulation of the problem:** The paper effectively explains the limitations of existing evaluation methods for Turkish.
*   **Open and accessible:** The public release of the benchmark on Hugging Face promotes collaboration and reproducibility.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited evaluation methodology:** The evaluation methods are relatively basic. A more in-depth analysis of model errors and the impact of different prompting strategies would have been valuable. The paper only mentions using a sentence similarity model to resolve differences in the answer format. A more detailed explanation of how this similarity model was trained and the associated thresholds used would improve the transparency and replicability of the results.
*   **Lack of comparative analysis with translated benchmarks:** While the paper criticizes translated benchmarks, a comparative evaluation of models on both TR-MMLU and translated versions of English benchmarks would have provided stronger empirical evidence for the limitations of the latter.
*   **The prompts used could use more explanation.** Though this is an evaluation, providing a small appendix of prompts for reference could be helpful for replicability purposes.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of 6. The creation of TR-MMLU is a valuable and necessary contribution, addressing a clear need for a specific benchmark for evaluating LLMs in the Turkish language. However, the paper's impact is somewhat limited by the relatively simple evaluation methodology and the lack of a deeper error analysis. This is partially offset by the publication of full model answers, which allows other researchers to engage in more detailed error analysis on their own. A rigorous evaluation process should consider a number of things, which were not necessarily considered in the publication. This includes things like the effect of length biases in answers and a better understanding of error distributions among the LLMs being evaluated. The overall impact also depends on the community's future adoption of TR-MMLU. In conclusion, the paper presents a relevant contribution to the Turkish NLP landscape but can be strengthened by improving the analysis aspect in future research.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Using AI for User Representation: An Analysis of 83 Persona Prompts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13047v1)
- **Authors**: Joni Salminen, Danial Amin, Bernard Jansen
- **Abstract**: We analyzed 83 persona prompts from 27 research articles that used large language models (LLMs) to generate user personas. Findings show that the prompts predominantly generate single personas. Several prompts express a desire for short or concise persona descriptions, which deviates from the tradition of creating rich, informative, and rounded persona profiles. Text is the most common format for generated persona attributes, followed by numbers. Text and numbers are often generated together, and demographic attributes are included in nearly all generated personas. Researchers use up to 12 prompts in a single study, though most research uses a small number of prompts. Comparison and testing multiple LLMs is rare. More than half of the prompts require the persona output in a structured format, such as JSON, and 74% of the prompts insert data or dynamic variables. We discuss the implications of increased use of computational personas for user representation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents an analysis of 83 persona prompts extracted from 27 research articles that leverage Large Language Models (LLMs) for persona generation. It explores the motivations behind using LLMs for this purpose, how researchers structure their prompts, and the characteristics of the personas generated. Key findings include the prevalence of single persona generation, a trend towards concise persona descriptions, the dominant use of text and numbers for attributes, and frequent inclusion of demographic information. The analysis also reveals that most studies use a small number of prompts, rarely compare multiple LLMs, and often require structured output (e.g., JSON). The paper concludes by discussing the implications of this shift towards computational personas for user representation, highlighting both opportunities and risks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a timely and relevant issue: the increasing use of LLMs in persona development. While the field of data-driven personas is established, the systematic analysis of *prompts* used in LLM-based persona generation is a relatively novel contribution. There has been work on how to build and use personas, and work on how LLMs can be used to generate text, but the intersection specifically analyzing prompts is less explored. The paper identifies a gap in the literature – a lack of systematic understanding of persona prompts - and attempts to fill it.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in providing a structured overview of current practices and identifying potential concerns.  By categorizing and analyzing prompts, the paper offers insights into how researchers are approaching LLM-based persona generation. This is valuable for researchers entering the field as well as those seeking to improve their own methodologies. The discussion of implications, such as the potential for reduced transparency and human agency, raises important ethical considerations. The paper highlights shifts, like moving towards shorter and more quantifiable personas, that diverge from the traditional rich qualitative ideal.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Systematic Approach:** The methodology, based on a systematic literature review and coding process, provides a solid foundation for the analysis.
    *   **Clear Research Questions:** The research questions are well-defined and directly address the identified gap in the literature.
    *   **Comprehensive Analysis:** The analysis covers various aspects of persona prompts, including their purpose, structure, and the characteristics of the generated personas.
    *   **Timeliness:** The paper tackles a current trend in HCI and related fields.
    *   **Reproducibility/Reusability:** Public availability of prompt collection enhances reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** The analysis is limited to *explicit* persona prompts found in research articles. It might not capture the nuances and variations in prompts used in practice or within industrial settings (which may be considered proprietary).
    *   **Descriptive Nature:** While the analysis is systematic, it is primarily descriptive. The paper does not provide strong empirical evidence on the *effects* of different prompting strategies on the quality or usefulness of the resulting personas.  This makes it difficult to draw definitive conclusions about best practices.
    *   **Lack of Comparative Evaluation:** The rare comparison of multiple LLMs is reflected in the analysis. There's little discussion of how to choose the right LLM for the task, or how the specific strengths of different models affect persona creation.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Grounding in Prompt Engineering:** While the paper notes general prompt engineering guidelines, it could benefit from a stronger connection to prompt engineering theory and best practices for LLMs. This could enrich the discussion of prompt design choices and their potential impact.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper serves as a valuable starting point for future research. It lays the groundwork for more in-depth investigations into the effectiveness of different prompting strategies and the potential biases introduced by LLM-based persona generation. It can also influence best practices in this domain.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of 7 because the paper offers a timely and systematic exploration of persona prompts, a relatively unexplored area. The paper successfully identifies and fills a gap in our understanding of how LLMs are being used in persona development. However, it is largely descriptive.  Future research building on this work could be more impactful by delving deeper into the impact of specific prompt design on persona quality. Also, by providing a more nuanced understanding of persona theory and prompt engineering theory.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### MAJIC: Markovian Adaptive Jailbreaking via Iterative Composition of Diverse Innovative Strategies
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13048v1)
- **Authors**: Weiwei Qi, Shuo Shao, Wei Gu, Tianhang Zheng, Puning Zhao, Zhan Qin, Kui Ren
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have exhibited remarkable capabilities but remain vulnerable to jailbreaking attacks, which can elicit harmful content from the models by manipulating the input prompts. Existing black-box jailbreaking techniques primarily rely on static prompts crafted with a single, non-adaptive strategy, or employ rigid combinations of several underperforming attack methods, which limits their adaptability and generalization. To address these limitations, we propose MAJIC, a Markovian adaptive jailbreaking framework that attacks black-box LLMs by iteratively combining diverse innovative disguise strategies. MAJIC first establishes a ``Disguise Strategy Pool'' by refining existing strategies and introducing several innovative approaches. To further improve the attack performance and efficiency, MAJIC formulate the sequential selection and fusion of strategies in the pool as a Markov chain. Under this formulation, MAJIC initializes and employs a Markov matrix to guide the strategy composition, where transition probabilities between strategies are dynamically adapted based on attack outcomes, thereby enabling MAJIC to learn and discover effective attack pathways tailored to the target model. Our empirical results demonstrate that MAJIC significantly outperforms existing jailbreak methods on prominent models such as GPT-4o and Gemini-2.0-flash, achieving over 90\% attack success rate with fewer than 15 queries per attempt on average.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "MAJIC: Markovian Adaptive Jailbreaking via Iterative Composition of Diverse Innovative Strategies" introduces a new black-box jailbreaking attack framework for Large Language Models (LLMs).  MAJIC utilizes a Markov chain to adaptively select and combine diverse "disguise strategies" to bypass safety mechanisms.  The framework consists of three main components: a "Disguise Strategy Pool" of both refined existing and novel attack strategies (contextual assumption, linguistic obfuscation, role-playing framing, semantic inversion, and literary disguise);  an initialization of a Markov Transition Matrix using a proxy LLM and local datasets to encode prior knowledge of strategy transitions; and a Q-learning-inspired dynamic strategy selection and adaptation process that updates the transition matrix based on the target LLM's responses during the attack.  Experiments on several state-of-the-art LLMs (including Gemini, GPT, and Claude) demonstrate that MAJIC outperforms existing jailbreaking methods in terms of attack success rate and query efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using a Markov chain for adaptive strategy selection in jailbreaking attacks is novel.  While combining strategies for jailbreaking has been explored, the dynamic, feedback-driven nature of MAJIC's Markovian approach, combined with a relatively large and diverse strategy pool, distinguishes it from previous work. The introduction of novel disguise strategies like "semantic inversion" and "literary disguise" adds to the novelty. It's a step up from static prompt engineering or simple iterative refinement of a single prompt. However, the Q-learning inspired update to the Markov chain, while sensible, is a fairly standard reinforcement learning application.

*   **Significance:** Jailbreaking LLMs is a significant problem with important security and ethical implications.  MAJIC's ability to achieve high success rates, especially against models like Claude-3.5-Sonnet that are known for their robust safety alignment, underscores its importance. The method's query efficiency is also significant, as it reduces the computational burden on the attacker and can potentially make attacks more difficult to detect. The modularity of the framework, allowing for the easy addition of new strategies, enhances its long-term significance. Also, the inclusion of the code for this framework increases the signficance, as well as allowing further investigation.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper presents compelling experimental results across a variety of LLMs and datasets, demonstrating MAJIC's superior performance compared to existing methods.
    *   **Adaptive Framework:** The Markovian approach allows MAJIC to adapt to different target models and evolving defenses.
    *   **Modular Design:** The Disguise Strategy Pool is modular and extensible, allowing for the incorporation of new attack strategies.
    *   **Query Efficiency:** MAJIC achieves high success rates with relatively few queries, which is important for practical attacks and for evading detection.
    *   **Detailed Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component of MAJIC.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** The framework involves several components (Markov chain, transition matrix initialization, Q-learning-inspired updates, and a range of disguise strategies), which can make it complex to implement and understand.
    *   **Dependence on Proxy LLM:** The initialization of the Markov transition matrix relies on a proxy LLM (LLaMA3-8B-Instruct). The choice of this proxy could potentially influence the performance of MAJIC. While the authors motivate this choice, a more thorough investigation into the sensitivity of MAJIC to the proxy model would be beneficial.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** The paper focuses primarily on empirical results, and it lacks a rigorous theoretical analysis of the convergence properties of the Q-learning-inspired update mechanism and the conditions under which the Markov chain is guaranteed to find effective attack sequences.
    *   **Scalability of Strategy Pool:** As the strategy pool grows, there is an increasing problem to effectively assess and update the corresponding transition probabilities, so additional mechanism to control the size of strategy pool will be very useful.

*   **Impact:** The paper is likely to have a significant impact on the field of LLM security.  It provides a new and effective approach to jailbreaking LLMs that can be used to identify vulnerabilities and develop more robust defenses.  The findings will be of interest to researchers and practitioners working on LLM safety, security, and alignment. The release of the code should further accelerate research in this area.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

MAJIC represents a significant advancement in black-box jailbreaking attacks on LLMs due to its novel Markovian approach, diverse strategy pool, and strong empirical results.  The framework is adaptive, modular, and query-efficient, making it a practical and effective attack method. While the complexity of the framework, dependence on a proxy LLM, limited theoretical analysis, and scalability of the strategy pool represent weaknesses, they are outweighed by the paper's significant contributions to the field of LLM security. Therefore, a score of 8 is assigned to reflect the overall impact and novelty of the paper.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Doğal Dil İşlemede Tokenizasyon Standartları ve Ölçümü: Türkçe Üzerinden Büyük Dil Modellerinin Karşılaştırmalı Analizi
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13058v1)
- **Authors**: M. Ali Bayram, Ali Arda Fincan, Ahmet Semih Gümüş, Sercan Karakaş, Banu Diri, Savaş Yıldırım
- **Abstract**: Tokenization is a fundamental preprocessing step in Natural Language Processing (NLP), significantly impacting the capability of large language models (LLMs) to capture linguistic and semantic nuances. This study introduces a novel evaluation framework addressing tokenization challenges specific to morphologically-rich and low-resource languages such as Turkish. Utilizing the Turkish MMLU (TR-MMLU) dataset, comprising 6,200 multiple-choice questions from the Turkish education system, we assessed tokenizers based on vocabulary size, token count, processing time, language-specific token percentages (\%TR), and token purity (\%Pure). These newly proposed metrics measure how effectively tokenizers preserve linguistic structures. Our analysis reveals that language-specific token percentages exhibit a stronger correlation with downstream performance (e.g., MMLU scores) than token purity. Furthermore, increasing model parameters alone does not necessarily enhance linguistic performance, underscoring the importance of tailored, language-specific tokenization methods. The proposed framework establishes robust and practical tokenization standards for morphologically complex languages.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper.

**Summary:**

The paper "Tokenization Standards and Evaluation in Natural Language Processing: A Comparative Analysis of Large Language Models on Turkish" addresses the challenges of tokenization for morphologically rich and low-resource languages, specifically Turkish. The authors propose a new evaluation framework using the Turkish MMLU (TR-MMLU) dataset and introduce new metrics: language-specific token percentage (%TR) and token purity (%Pure). The study analyzes the performance of different tokenization methods based on vocabulary size, token count, processing time, %TR, and %Pure. The findings suggest that a higher language-specific token percentage is more strongly correlated with downstream task performance than token purity. Moreover, the paper highlights that increasing model parameters alone does not guarantee better linguistic performance and that language-specific tokenization methods are crucial. The paper emphasizes the importance of balancing computational efficiency and linguistic accuracy in tokenization strategies for morphologically complex languages.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its evaluation framework and the introduction of the %TR and %Pure metrics, specifically tailored for morphologically rich languages. While the concept of evaluating tokenizers is not new, the application of these metrics to the Turkish language and the TR-MMLU dataset provides a targeted approach. The contribution of TR MMLU itself is significant because of the gap it fills in Turkish benchmarks, as noted in the authors' previous work. However, the individual components of the framework, such as using token count and processing time as metrics, are fairly standard in tokenizer evaluation. The evaluation using TR-MMLU to drive conclusions specifically relating to Turkish NLP also has some novelty.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance is moderate. It provides a practical framework for evaluating tokenization strategies in morphologically complex languages, which can guide the development of more effective tokenizers for these languages. The finding that language-specific token percentage is more important than token purity is valuable and suggests that capturing the linguistic nuances of the target language is crucial for downstream task performance. Also, the observation that simply increasing model parameters doesn't override the need for dedicated tokenizers is an important point.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-Defined Metrics:** The introduction of %TR and %Pure metrics offers a more nuanced way to assess tokenization performance than simply relying on token count or vocabulary size.
    *   **Relevant Dataset:** The use of the TR-MMLU dataset provides a realistic and challenging benchmark for evaluating tokenizers on Turkish.
    *   **Clear Analysis:** The paper presents a clear and concise analysis of the experimental results, highlighting the strengths and weaknesses of different tokenization strategies.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The findings have practical implications for developing and selecting tokenizers for Turkish NLP tasks.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** The study focuses solely on Turkish. While the framework is presented as generalizable, its applicability to other languages needs to be demonstrated. The number of evaluated tokenizers is also limited to just four, which may not be fully representative of the available options.
    *   **Incremental Contribution:** While the %TR and %Pure metrics are useful additions, they build upon existing evaluation methods and might be considered incremental rather than groundbreaking.
    *   **Potential for Deeper Linguistic Analysis:** The paper could benefit from a more in-depth linguistic analysis of the tokenization errors and their impact on downstream task performance. This might involve a qualitative assessment of the tokenization outputs to understand the types of linguistic structures that are not being adequately captured. The discussion on error examples could have benefited from more detailed linguistic explanation.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of tokenization strategies for morphologically rich languages and can serve as a reference for future research in this area. The TR-MMLU dataset and the proposed evaluation framework can become valuable resources for the Turkish NLP community.

**Justification for the Score:**

Considering the paper's strengths and weaknesses, I assign a score of **6.5**. The paper presents a useful evaluation framework for tokenization in morphologically rich languages, specifically addressing Turkish. It introduces novel metrics that offer a more targeted way to assess tokenizer performance. However, the scope is limited to a single language, the contribution is somewhat incremental, and there's room for deeper linguistic analysis. The study provides practical guidance and valuable resources for the Turkish NLP community. The influence may not be revolutionary, but it's a solid step forward in the area.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Reinforced Context Order Recovery for Adaptive Reasoning and Planning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13070v1)
- **Authors**: Long Ma, Fangwei Zhong, Yizhou Wang
- **Abstract**: Modern causal language models, followed by rapid developments in discrete diffusion models, can now produce a wide variety of interesting and useful content. However, these families of models are predominantly trained to output tokens with a fixed (left-to-right) or random order, which may deviate from the logical order in which tokens are generated originally. In this paper, we observe that current causal and diffusion models encounter difficulties in problems that require adaptive token generation orders to solve tractably, which we characterize with the $\mathcal{V}$-information framework. Motivated by this, we propose Reinforced Context Order Recovery (ReCOR), a reinforcement-learning-based framework to extract adaptive, data-dependent token generation orders from text data without annotations. Self-supervised by token prediction statistics, ReCOR estimates the hardness of predicting every unfilled token and adaptively selects the next token during both training and inference. Experiments on challenging reasoning and planning datasets demonstrate the superior performance of ReCOR compared with baselines, sometimes outperforming oracle models supervised with the ground-truth order.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Reinforced Context Order Recovery (ReCOR), a novel reinforcement learning-based framework for adaptive token generation order in text generation. ReCOR addresses the limitation of current causal language models (CLMs) and diffusion models that rely on fixed token generation orders (left-to-right or random), which can be suboptimal for complex reasoning and planning tasks. ReCOR learns to predict the hardness of predicting each unfilled token and adaptively selects the next token during training and inference, without requiring explicit order annotations. The framework is self-supervised using token prediction statistics.  The paper demonstrates ReCOR's effectiveness on various reasoning and planning datasets, showing superior performance compared to baselines and sometimes even outperforming oracle models trained with ground-truth orders. The approach leverages the V-information framework to characterize token hardness, optimizing a cumulative predictive V-information objective.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of learning an adaptive token generation order using reinforcement learning in a self-supervised manner is a significant contribution. While prior work has explored adaptive inference in diffusion models, ReCOR's approach of learning the order during *both* training and inference, and without relying on diffusion models, sets it apart. The introduction and use of the V-information framework, although not entirely new, provides a useful theoretical lens for understanding and formalizing the problem of token hardness. The paper effectively casts order recovery as a decision-making problem.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial limitation of current text generation models regarding their ability to tackle complex reasoning and planning tasks, which are inherently non-linear and require flexible decision-making. By allowing the model to choose the order in which tokens are generated, ReCOR unlocks potential benefits for a wide range of applications. The experimental results clearly demonstrate the effectiveness of ReCOR, outperforming strong baselines and even oracle models in some cases. The ablation studies and analyses provide valuable insights into the design choices and the importance of learning the order during both training and inference.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined problem statement and clear motivation.
    *   The novel approach of using RL for learning adaptive token order.
    *   Self-supervised learning avoids the need for costly annotations.
    *   Strong empirical results, demonstrating improved performance on challenging tasks.
    *   Thorough ablation studies providing useful insights.
    *   The design of a multistream architecture is well reasoned and enhances model expressiveness.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The use of the V-information framework, while helpful, could be expanded upon. A more detailed analysis of how well the V-information metric correlates with actual token prediction difficulty would strengthen the argument.
    *   While the performance improvements are significant, the computational cost of ReCOR compared to standard CLMs should be addressed more explicitly. The paper mentions KV-caching, but a direct comparison of training and inference times would be valuable.
    *   The choice of RL algorithm (Soft Q-learning) seems adequate, but the paper could explore other RL algorithms and justify the selection in more detail.
    *   The experiments are mostly limited to reasoning and planning tasks that rely on discrete token spaces. It would be beneficial to explore the effectiveness of ReCOR on more diverse text generation tasks.

*   **Potential Impact:** ReCOR has the potential to significantly impact the field of text generation by enabling more robust and flexible reasoning and planning capabilities. The self-supervised nature of the approach makes it particularly attractive for real-world applications where labeled data is scarce.  The method's potential influence lies in the ability to overcome limitations of fixed order processing in handling complex, informationally interdependent data sequences, applicable not just to language but potentially other sequential data domains. Future research can build upon ReCOR by exploring different RL algorithms, scaling it to larger models and datasets, and extending it to more complex tasks.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:** ReCOR demonstrates strong novelty in the application of reinforcement learning for adaptive token order prediction and offers a significant improvement over existing text generation methods, especially for reasoning and planning tasks. The self-supervised nature of the approach and the empirical results contribute to its potential impact. While there are some weaknesses in terms of computational cost analysis and limitations on certain aspects of the framework, the overall contribution is substantial, making it a valuable advance in the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### From Transthoracic to Transesophageal: Cross-Modality Generation using LoRA Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13077v1)
- **Authors**: Emmanuel Oladokun, Yuxuan Ou, Anna Novikova, Daria Kulikova, Sarina Thomas, Jurica Šprem, Vicente Grau
- **Abstract**: Deep diffusion models excel at realistic image synthesis but demand large training sets-an obstacle in data-scarce domains like transesophageal echocardiography (TEE). While synthetic augmentation has boosted performance in transthoracic echo (TTE), TEE remains critically underrepresented, limiting the reach of deep learning in this high-impact modality. We address this gap by adapting a TTE-trained, mask-conditioned diffusion backbone to TEE with only a limited number of new cases and adapters as small as $10^5$ parameters. Our pipeline combines Low-Rank Adaptation with MaskR$^2$, a lightweight remapping layer that aligns novel mask formats with the pretrained model's conditioning channels. This design lets users adapt models to new datasets with a different set of anatomical structures to the base model's original set. Through a targeted adaptation strategy, we find that adapting only MLP layers suffices for high-fidelity TEE synthesis. Finally, mixing less than 200 real TEE frames with our synthetic echoes improves the dice score on a multiclass segmentation task, particularly boosting performance on underrepresented right-heart structures. Our results demonstrate that (1) semantically controlled TEE images can be generated with low overhead, (2) MaskR$^2$ effectively transforms unseen mask formats into compatible formats without damaging downstream task performance, and (3) our method generates images that are effective for improving performance on a downstream task of multiclass segmentation.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the data scarcity issue in transesophageal echocardiography (TEE) image analysis by proposing a cross-modality generation pipeline. They adapt a TTE-trained diffusion model to TEE using Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) and a novel "MaskR2" layer.  MaskR2 remaps new semantic maps (TEE labels) into the expected input format of the pre-trained TTE model without requiring retraining of the entire network. They show that this approach can generate realistic synthetic TEE images, even with limited TEE data, and that augmenting real TEE datasets with these synthetic images improves performance on a downstream multi-class segmentation task, especially for underrepresented right-heart structures.  The paper demonstrates that adapting only MLP layers is sufficient for high-fidelity TEE synthesis, making it efficient.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper possesses a moderate degree of novelty.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The application of LoRA for cross-modality adaptation in echocardiography is potentially novel. While LoRA itself isn't new, its specific application to bridging the gap between TTE and TEE, particularly with a focus on data augmentation, is a valuable exploration.
    *   The "MaskR2" layer is a key contribution. The paper highlights its utility in adapting conditioning channels when moving between datasets with different label sets. It is a practical solution to a common problem when fine-tuning generative models across different domains. This allows them to leverage a pre-trained network, even with different sets of anatomical structures present in the new dataset.
    *   The targeted adaptation strategy, i.e., determining which layers are most critical for adapting to echo data, is a useful investigation that can inform future work in this area. The finding that adapting only MLP layers suffices is a significant efficiency gain.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Diffusion models themselves are a well-established technology, and LoRA is an existing adaptation technique.  The work builds upon these foundations, rather than presenting a completely revolutionary approach.
    *   While MaskR2 is useful, it is a relatively simple solution and may not be applicable to more complex adaptation scenarios beyond semantic masks with a relatively simple relationship between different structure sets. Its scope might be limited.

**Significance:** The significance lies in its potential to improve the availability and quality of TEE image analysis, a modality that suffers from data scarcity.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The demonstrated improvement in segmentation performance, particularly for the underrepresented right-heart structures, is a strong practical outcome. This directly addresses a clinical need, as accurate right-heart assessment is crucial in many cardiac conditions.
    *   The efficiency of the method (using LoRA and only adapting MLP layers) makes it more accessible and practical for researchers and clinicians who may lack extensive computational resources.
    *   The public availability of SSMs enhances the reproducibility and adoption of the proposed method.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper lacks a more rigorous comparison against other data augmentation techniques (e.g., GAN-based methods). While they briefly mention GANs, a more direct comparison would strengthen the justification for using their proposed approach.
    *   The image quality metrics used (FID, LPIPS) have acknowledged limitations when evaluating medical images, particularly echocardiograms. A more tailored evaluation metric, or a user study with clinicians, would provide a more robust assessment of the generated image quality.
    *   The evaluation is based on a single downstream task (segmentation). Evaluating the generated images on other clinically relevant tasks (e.g., disease classification, anomaly detection) would further demonstrate their utility.
    *   The improvement in Dice scores, though positive, is relatively modest in some cases, suggesting that there is room for further optimization.

**Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in cross-modality adaptation for medical imaging. The specific findings on LoRA, MaskR2, and the targeted adaptation strategy can guide the development of more efficient and effective data augmentation techniques for other data-scarce medical imaging modalities. It encourages exploration of diffusion models, coupled with adaptation strategies, for improving diagnostic accuracy in settings where data is scarce.

**Justification of Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **7**.

*   The paper tackles a relevant problem with a practical and efficient approach. The use of LoRA and MaskR2 demonstrates a good understanding of current machine learning techniques.
*   The achieved results in segmentation improvement are significant, especially for underrepresented regions.
*   However, the paper lacks a strong, comprehensive comparison against other state-of-the-art data augmentation techniques. While the approach is sound, the innovation rests primarily in applying existing techniques to a new problem domain (TEE imaging) and implementing an elegant data adaptation layer, MaskR2. A more thorough investigation that considers established baselines and customized metrics designed for evaluating synthesized medical images could substantially boost the impact of the study.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 7/10

### DMS:Diffusion-Based Multi-Baseline Stereo Generation for Improving Self-Supervised Depth Estimation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13091v1)
- **Authors**: Zihua Liu, Yizhou Li, Songyan Zhang, Masatoshi Okutomi
- **Abstract**: While supervised stereo matching and monocular depth estimation have advanced significantly with learning-based algorithms, self-supervised methods using stereo images as supervision signals have received relatively less focus and require further investigation. A primary challenge arises from ambiguity introduced during photometric reconstruction, particularly due to missing corresponding pixels in ill-posed regions of the target view, such as occlusions and out-of-frame areas. To address this and establish explicit photometric correspondences, we propose DMS, a model-agnostic approach that utilizes geometric priors from diffusion models to synthesize novel views along the epipolar direction, guided by directional prompts. Specifically, we finetune a Stable Diffusion model to simulate perspectives at key positions: left-left view shifted from the left camera, right-right view shifted from the right camera, along with an additional novel view between the left and right cameras. These synthesized views supplement occluded pixels, enabling explicit photometric reconstruction. Our proposed DMS is a cost-free, ''plug-and-play'' method that seamlessly enhances self-supervised stereo matching and monocular depth estimation, and relies solely on unlabeled stereo image pairs for both training and synthesizing. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of our approach, with up to 35% outlier reduction and state-of-the-art performance across multiple benchmark datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DMS (Diffusion-based Multi-Baseline Stereo Generation), a novel, model-agnostic approach to improve self-supervised depth estimation. DMS leverages geometric priors from latent diffusion models (LDMs) to synthesize additional viewpoints along the epipolar line, effectively extending the stereo baseline. By fine-tuning a Stable Diffusion model with directional prompts (e.g., "to left," "to right"), DMS generates left-left, right-right, and intermediate views. These synthesized views augment the original stereo pair, providing more explicit photometric correspondences and reducing ambiguities in occluded and out-of-frame regions. The method is "plug-and-play," requiring only unlabeled stereo image pairs for training and synthesis. Experiments demonstrate significant outlier reduction and state-of-the-art performance across several benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper exhibits a moderate degree of novelty. While self-supervised depth estimation and multi-baseline stereo setups have been explored individually, the combination of large-scale latent diffusion models with directional prompts to *synthesize* multi-baseline stereo images as a pre-processing step for depth estimation is relatively new. Prior approaches typically rely on physically-existing multi-camera systems or geometric methods with limited accuracy for generating such views.
