### ALIGN: Word Association Learning for Cross-Cultural Generalization in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13426v1)
- **Authors**: Chunhua Liu, Kabir Manandhar Shrestha, Sukai Huang
- **Abstract**: As large language models (LLMs) increasingly mediate cross-cultural communication, their behavior still reflects the distributional bias of the languages and viewpoints that are over-represented in their pre-training corpora. Yet, it remains a challenge to model and align culture due to limited cultural knowledge and a lack of exploration into effective learning approaches. We introduce a cost-efficient, cognitively grounded remedy: parameter-efficient fine-tuning on native speakers' free word-association norms, which encode implicit cultural schemas. Leveraging English-US and Mandarin associations from the Small-World-of-Words project, we adapt Llama-3.1-8B and Qwen-2.5-7B via supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and PPO-based preference optimization. SFT boosts held-out association Precision at 5 by 16-20% in English and 43-165% in Mandarin, lifts median concreteness by +0.20, and attains human-level valence and arousal. These lexical gains transfer: on World-Values-Survey questions, fine-tuned models shift answer distributions toward the target culture, and on a 50-item high-tension subset, Qwen's Chinese-aligned responses double while Llama's US bias drops by one-third. Our 7-8B models rival or beat vanilla 70B baselines, showing that a few million culture-grounded associations can instill value alignment without costly retraining. Our work highlights both the promise and the need for future research grounded in human cognition in improving cultural alignment in AI models.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ALIGN, a method for improving cross-cultural generalization in large language models (LLMs) through parameter-efficient fine-tuning on native speakers’ free word-association norms. The approach leverages English-US and Mandarin associations from the Small-World-of-Words project to fine-tune LLAMA-3.1-8B and QWEN-2.5-7B using supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and PPO-based preference optimization. The results show that SFT boosts association precision significantly, improves concreteness, and achieves human-level valence and arousal. These lexical gains translate into better alignment with target cultures on World-Values-Survey questions, demonstrating the potential for value alignment without costly retraining. The work highlights the promise and need for future research grounded in human cognition to improve cultural alignment in AI models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its use of word association norms as a direct, culturally grounded resource for fine-tuning LLMs. While prior works have explored cultural alignment using survey-based methods, synthetic data generation, or linear transformation of word embeddings, this is among the first papers to train word association norms directly to adjust LLM behavior. Another key novel aspect is the head-to-head comparison of SFT and PPO for injecting cultural knowledge via such associations.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work is threefold: it provides a cost-effective method for improving cross-cultural generalization in LLMs; it demonstrates the effectiveness of leveraging human cognitive data for AI alignment; and it offers an open-source resource (LoRA adapters) to allow the community to inject US- or CN-specific cultural knowledge into their models. The findings have implications for fostering diversity and effective communication in global AI ecosystems.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and well-motivated approach.
    *   Rigorous experimentation with appropriate baselines and evaluation metrics.
    *   Use of publicly available and high-quality datasets (SWOW and WVS).
    *   Demonstration of transferability from lexical-level knowledge to higher-level value judgments.
    *   Code and data release (upon acceptance) for reproducibility and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The study is limited to two languages (English and Mandarin) and two specific models (Llama-3.1-8B and Qwen-2.5-7B).
    *   The evaluation focuses on national-level cultural values and does not account for regional, social, or demographic variations within countries.
    *   There is a temporal gap between the collection of the human data and the training data for the LLMs.
    *   The performance improvements, while significant, are not uniform across all evaluation tasks and metrics. While the authors test "tension" questions in one of their evaluations, it is difficult to claim these few instances completely transfer across large populations.
    *   Word associations may be influenced by things besides cultural values (like personal history, or even just random chance in responding) so direct training on them may inject noise that isn't purely "cultural knowledge."

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research on cultural alignment in LLMs, encouraging the use of cognitively grounded data and parameter-efficient fine-tuning techniques. The resource release is likely to be widely adopted by the community. While not mentioned in the text, there are potential ethical concerns here as this work has the potential to reinforce existing stereotypes.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper represents a solid contribution to the field of cultural alignment in LLMs. The use of word association norms is a novel and promising approach, and the experimental results are compelling. While the study has some limitations in scope and potential for overfitting, the potential influence of the work and its contribution to a more diverse and inclusive AI ecosystem justify a score of 7.5.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Structured Prompting and Multi-Agent Knowledge Distillation for Traffic Video Interpretation and Risk Inference
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13439v1)
- **Authors**: Yunxiang Yang, Ningning Xu, Jidong J. Yang
- **Abstract**: Comprehensive highway scene understanding and robust traffic risk inference are vital for advancing Intelligent Transportation Systems (ITS) and autonomous driving. Traditional approaches often struggle with scalability and generalization, particularly under the complex and dynamic conditions of real-world environments. To address these challenges, we introduce a novel structured prompting and knowledge distillation framework that enables automatic generation of high-quality traffic scene annotations and contextual risk assessments. Our framework orchestrates two large Vision-Language Models (VLMs): GPT-4o and o3-mini, using a structured Chain-of-Thought (CoT) strategy to produce rich, multi-perspective outputs. These outputs serve as knowledge-enriched pseudo-annotations for supervised fine-tuning of a much smaller student VLM. The resulting compact 3B-scale model, named VISTA (Vision for Intelligent Scene and Traffic Analysis), is capable of understanding low-resolution traffic videos and generating semantically faithful, risk-aware captions. Despite its significantly reduced parameter count, VISTA achieves strong performance across established captioning metrics (BLEU-4, METEOR, ROUGE-L, and CIDEr) when benchmarked against its teacher models. This demonstrates that effective knowledge distillation and structured multi-agent supervision can empower lightweight VLMs to capture complex reasoning capabilities. The compact architecture of VISTA facilitates efficient deployment on edge devices, enabling real-time risk monitoring without requiring extensive infrastructure upgrades.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework for traffic video interpretation and risk inference called VISTA (Vision for Intelligent Scene and Traffic Analysis). This framework uses a multi-agent knowledge distillation approach, leveraging two large vision-language models (VLMs): GPT-4o and o3-mini. These models are orchestrated using a structured Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting strategy to generate rich, multi-perspective outputs, effectively creating knowledge-enriched pseudo-annotations. These pseudo-annotations are then used to fine-tune a smaller, more efficient 3B-scale student VLM (VISTA). The distilled VISTA model can understand low-resolution traffic videos and generate risk-aware captions, enabling real-time risk monitoring without extensive infrastructure upgrades.  The paper presents experimental results demonstrating that VISTA achieves strong performance across established captioning metrics compared to its teacher models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several innovative aspects. The use of multi-agent knowledge distillation, specifically orchestrating GPT-4o and o3-mini through structured CoT prompting for traffic video analysis, is a noteworthy contribution. The idea of generating high-quality pseudo-annotations for supervised fine-tuning a lightweight model is also valuable. It distinguishes itself from traditional knowledge distillation methods which focus on feature/logit transfer. The application of VLMs to the specific problem of low-resolution traffic video analysis and risk inference also adds to the novelty. However, CoT and knowledge distillation in themselves are not entirely new, and this is more a clever integration and tailoring of these techniques for a specific application.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work lies in its potential for real-world deployment. The development of a lightweight VLM (3B scale) that can perform complex tasks like traffic scene understanding and risk assessment opens up possibilities for edge deployment and real-time monitoring on existing infrastructure. This reduces the need for extensive hardware upgrades, making the technology more accessible. This aspect directly tackles a critical challenge in ITS: the limitations imposed by computational constraints and infrastructure availability. The experimental results support the claim that the distilled model achieves competitive performance, strengthening the potential impact. The focus on generating actionable insights, like driver alerts and suggested speeds, further enhances the practical value of the system. However, the paper should have conducted more analysis to compare its performance with existing methods in traffic safety and risk inference, specifically, to provide stronger evidence of its advantages.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined methodology with a multi-agent CoT prompting and knowledge distillation approach.
    *   Demonstrated effectiveness of the distilled model (VISTA) in terms of captioning metrics.
    *   Focus on practical deployment through a lightweight architecture.
    *   Comprehensive experiments and Ablation studies to measure the contribution of each component.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The novelty is incremental rather than revolutionary. While the integration of techniques is clever, the underlying methods are established.
    *   Limited comparison to existing methods in traffic risk assessment.
    *   The reliance on proprietary models like GPT-4o could be a concern regarding reproducibility and accessibility for other researchers.
    *   The reliance on public webcam data might limit the study and can bring new challenges for generalization in different traffic scenarios.
    *   Lack of clarity on computational resources for prompt training and inferencing.
    *   Additional experiments are needed to evaluate the robustness of the proposed method.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper's potential impact is considerable. The VISTA framework could contribute to the development of more efficient and accessible traffic monitoring systems, potentially leading to improved road safety and reduced traffic congestion. The methodology may inspire the use of similar multi-agent knowledge distillation techniques in other resource-constrained domains. However, broader adoption hinges on validating the system's reliability and performance in various real-world scenarios and datasets.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7** to this paper.

The paper is a solid piece of research that presents a practical and well-executed approach to traffic video analysis and risk inference. The multi-agent knowledge distillation framework is innovative in its specific application and demonstrates the feasibility of creating lightweight VLMs for resource-constrained environments. The focus on real-world deployment and actionable insights is commendable. However, the limitations related to incremental novelty, lack of thorough comparative analysis with existing methods, reliance on proprietary models, and broader validation efforts prevents it from achieving a higher score. The potential impact is significant, but further research and validation are necessary to realize its full potential.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Vision Transformers for Kidney Stone Image Classification: A Comparative Study with CNNs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13461v1)
- **Authors**: Ivan Reyes-Amezcua, Francisco Lopez-Tiro, Clement Larose, Andres Mendez-Vazquez, Gilberto Ochoa-Ruiz, Christian Daul
- **Abstract**: Kidney stone classification from endoscopic images is critical for personalized treatment and recurrence prevention. While convolutional neural networks (CNNs) have shown promise in this task, their limited ability to capture long-range dependencies can hinder performance under variable imaging conditions. This study presents a comparative analysis between Vision Transformers (ViTs) and CNN-based models, evaluating their performance on two ex vivo datasets comprising CCD camera and flexible ureteroscope images. The ViT-base model pretrained on ImageNet-21k consistently outperformed a ResNet50 baseline across multiple imaging conditions. For instance, in the most visually complex subset (Section patches from endoscopic images), the ViT model achieved 95.2% accuracy and 95.1% F1-score, compared to 64.5% and 59.3% with ResNet50. In the mixed-view subset from CCD-camera images, ViT reached 87.1% accuracy versus 78.4% with CNN. These improvements extend across precision and recall as well. The results demonstrate that ViT-based architectures provide superior classification performance and offer a scalable alternative to conventional CNNs for kidney stone image analysis.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper compares Vision Transformers (ViTs) and Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) for classifying kidney stone images acquired from endoscopic and CCD cameras. The authors train and test both ViT and CNN models on two ex vivo datasets, analyzing their performance in terms of accuracy, precision, recall, and F1-score. They also perform ablation studies to evaluate the impact of model size and pretraining strategies. The results demonstrate that ViT models, particularly those pretrained on ImageNet-21k, consistently outperform CNN-based architectures across multiple metrics and imaging conditions. The paper includes feature visualizations and intra-class distance analysis to understand the representational capabilities of both models. The paper concludes that ViTs offer superior classification performance and represent a scalable alternative to conventional CNNs for kidney stone image analysis.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comparative Analysis:** The paper presents a well-executed comparative study between ViTs and CNNs for kidney stone image classification, a task of clinical relevance.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The study includes a thorough evaluation of model performance using multiple metrics and ablation studies to investigate the impact of different training strategies and model architectures.
    *   **Detailed Analysis:** The paper goes beyond performance metrics by including feature visualizations and intra-class distance analysis to understand how the models represent the data and make decisions.
    *   **Clinical Significance:** The paper addresses a practical problem in urology, as precise kidney stone identification can lead to individualized treatment and prevent recurrence. The potential application of computer vision in this area makes the work practically relevant.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Novelty:** While the application of ViTs to kidney stone classification is interesting, ViTs have been successfully applied to medical imaging problems before. The novelty lies primarily in the specific application domain rather than in a fundamental algorithmic breakthrough.
    *   **Ex Vivo Data:** The study utilizes ex vivo datasets, which may not perfectly reflect the challenges encountered in real-time in vivo clinical settings, such as motion blur, varying illumination, and presence of biological fluids.
    *   **Specific Datasets:** The datasets are specific to CCD and flexible ureteroscope images, potentially limiting the generalization of the findings to other imaging modalities or datasets.
    *   **Limited Interpretability:** The visualization techniques used (t-SNE) provide some insight into feature representations, but further work could explore attention maps or other methods to provide more detailed and clinically relevant interpretability.
    *   **Missing comparison with specific state of the art for kidney stone analysis:** The work does not compare specifically against state-of-the-art techniques dedicated to kidney stone image analysis and based on CNNs (but perhaps with specific enhancements). Choosing a generic ResNet50 is a reasonable baseline, but not exhaustive.

*   **Significance:**
    *   The paper demonstrates the potential of ViTs for kidney stone image classification, showing they can outperform CNNs, particularly in challenging scenarios.
    *   The study provides insights into the representational capabilities of ViTs and CNNs in medical imaging, which could inform future research in this area.
    *   The work could contribute to the development of automated tools for kidney stone diagnosis and treatment planning.

*   **Justification for Score:**

The paper offers a solid comparative analysis of ViTs and CNNs for a specific medical imaging problem. The work is well-executed, clearly presented, and provides useful insights. However, the novelty is somewhat limited, as ViTs have been applied to medical imaging before, and the study relies on ex vivo data. The study is detailed, technically sound, and offers insights into the strengths and weaknesses of ViTs for this particular task. Given these considerations, the significance is present but the novelty is limited, thus the study makes a good but not outstanding contribution.

**Score: 6.5**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### From Scores to Skills: A Cognitive Diagnosis Framework for Evaluating Financial Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13491v1)
- **Authors**: Ziyan Kuang, Feiyu Zhu, Maowei Jiang, Yanzhao Lai, Zelin Wang, Zhitong Wang, Meikang Qiu, Jiajia Huang, Min Peng, Qianqian Xie, Sophia Ananiadou
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown promise for financial applications, yet their suitability for this high-stakes domain remains largely unproven due to inadequacies in existing benchmarks. Existing benchmarks solely rely on score-level evaluation, summarizing performance with a single score that obscures the nuanced understanding of what models truly know and their precise limitations. They also rely on datasets that cover only a narrow subset of financial concepts, while overlooking other essentials for real-world applications. To address these gaps, we introduce FinCDM, the first cognitive diagnosis evaluation framework tailored for financial LLMs, enabling the evaluation of LLMs at the knowledge-skill level, identifying what financial skills and knowledge they have or lack based on their response patterns across skill-tagged tasks, rather than a single aggregated number. We construct CPA-QKA, the first cognitively informed financial evaluation dataset derived from the Certified Public Accountant (CPA) examination, with comprehensive coverage of real-world accounting and financial skills. It is rigorously annotated by domain experts, who author, validate, and annotate questions with high inter-annotator agreement and fine-grained knowledge labels. Our extensive experiments on 30 proprietary, open-source, and domain-specific LLMs show that FinCDM reveals hidden knowledge gaps, identifies under-tested areas such as tax and regulatory reasoning overlooked by traditional benchmarks, and uncovers behavioral clusters among models. FinCDM introduces a new paradigm for financial LLM evaluation by enabling interpretable, skill-aware diagnosis that supports more trustworthy and targeted model development, and all datasets and evaluation scripts will be publicly released to support further research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "From Scores to Skills: A Cognitive Diagnosis Framework for Evaluating Financial Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FinCDM, a cognitive diagnosis framework for evaluating the financial knowledge and skills of Large Language Models (LLMs).  Unlike existing benchmarks that rely on aggregate scores, FinCDM aims to provide a more fine-grained understanding of what LLMs actually know and where their weaknesses lie.  The framework is based on Cognitive Diagnosis Models (CDMs) used in educational assessment and involves assessing LLMs' responses to skill-tagged financial tasks. The authors also present CPA-QKA, a new, rigorously annotated dataset derived from the Certified Public Accountant (CPA) exam, to support the FinCDM framework. The paper demonstrates the effectiveness of FinCDM by evaluating 30 LLMs and revealing hidden knowledge gaps, identifying overlooked areas in existing benchmarks, and uncovering behavioral clusters among the models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty of the paper lies in the application of Cognitive Diagnosis Models (CDMs) to the evaluation of financial LLMs.  While CDMs have been used extensively in educational assessment, their adoption for assessing LLMs in specialized domains like finance is a significant contribution. The creation of the CPA-QKA dataset, designed explicitly for knowledge-skill level evaluation, also adds to the paper's novelty.
*   **Significance:** The significance of this work is multi-fold. First, it addresses a critical gap in financial LLM evaluation: the lack of fine-grained diagnostics. By moving beyond aggregate scores, FinCDM enables a more nuanced understanding of LLM capabilities, identifying specific strengths and weaknesses. Second, the framework aids in targeted model development by highlighting areas where LLMs require improvement. Finally, the CPA-QKA dataset serves as a valuable resource for future research in financial LLM evaluation.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The use of CDMs provides a theoretically sound and interpretable approach to LLM evaluation.
    *   The CPA-QKA dataset is rigorously constructed and annotated, ensuring high quality and reliability.
    *   The extensive experiments on 30 LLMs provide a comprehensive assessment of the framework's effectiveness.
    *   The paper clearly demonstrates how FinCDM reveals insights that are missed by traditional benchmarks.
    *   The promise to publicly release the datasets and evaluation scripts promotes further research.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on expert annotations, while ensuring quality, could be a potential bottleneck for scaling the approach to larger datasets or broader financial domains. Automating or semi-automating the annotation process could be explored.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more detailed analysis of the specific types of errors made by the LLMs and the underlying reasons for those errors.
    *   While the paper discusses model specialization, it lacks a concrete demonstration of how the diagnostic information from FinCDM can be used to fine-tune or improve model performance.
    *   The generative model could benefit from exploring other options besides Gamma and Bernoulli.

*   **Potential Influence:** FinCDM has the potential to significantly influence the field of financial LLM evaluation by providing a more rigorous and interpretable approach to assessing model capabilities. The CPA-QKA dataset could also become a standard benchmark for evaluating financial LLMs, promoting more focused and effective model development. The framework's ability to identify specific skill gaps could guide the creation of more targeted pre-training or fine-tuning strategies. The framework allows for better trustworthiness and targeted model development.

*   **Rigorous Justification** The strength in this work lies in the ability to evaluate the granular cognitive performance of LLMs in finance, which is missed by score evaluations. The evaluation methodology is also sound and validated by domain experts and data-driven performance across a variety of LLMs. These contribute to the novelty and significant impact of the work.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

FinCDM represents a significant advance in the field of financial LLM evaluation. The use of CDMs is a novel and theoretically sound approach that provides valuable insights into model capabilities. The CPA-QKA dataset is a valuable resource, and the extensive experiments demonstrate the framework's effectiveness. While there are some limitations, such as the dependence on expert annotations, these do not detract significantly from the paper's overall contribution. Given the novelty, significance, and rigorous evaluation, a score of 8 is warranted. It demonstrates substantial impact but provides room for future extension with improvement and adaptation on the annotation requirements of a complex domain.

- **Classification**: cs.CE
- **Score**: 8/10

### LLM-Enhanced Linear Autoencoders for Recommendation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13500v1)
- **Authors**: Jaewan Moon, Seongmin Park, Jongwuk Lee
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have been widely adopted to enrich the semantic representation of textual item information in recommender systems. However, existing linear autoencoders (LAEs) that incorporate textual information rely on sparse word co-occurrence patterns, limiting their ability to capture rich textual semantics. To address this, we propose L3AE, the first integration of LLMs into the LAE framework. L3AE effectively integrates the heterogeneous knowledge of textual semantics and user-item interactions through a two-phase optimization strategy. (i) L3AE first constructs a semantic item-to-item correlation matrix from LLM-derived item representations. (ii) It then learns an item-to-item weight matrix from collaborative signals while distilling semantic item correlations as regularization. Notably, each phase of L3AE is optimized through closed-form solutions, ensuring global optimality and computational efficiency. Extensive experiments demonstrate that L3AE consistently outperforms state-of-the-art LLM-enhanced models on three benchmark datasets, achieving gains of 27.6% in Recall@20 and 39.3% in NDCG@20. The source code is available at https://github.com/jaewan7599/L3AE_CIKM2025.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces L³AE, a novel approach to recommendation systems that integrates Large Language Models (LLMs) into the Linear Autoencoder (LAE) framework.  The method aims to improve the representation of items, especially long-tail items, by leveraging LLMs to capture rich semantic information. L³AE operates in two phases: first, it constructs a semantic item-to-item correlation matrix from LLM-derived embeddings; second, it learns an item-to-item weight matrix from user-item interactions, regularized by the semantic correlation.  The authors claim that both phases have closed-form solutions, ensuring global optimality and computational efficiency.  Experiments on three Amazon review datasets demonstrate that L³AE outperforms existing LLM-enhanced and traditional recommendation models, particularly on long-tail items.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the *specific* way the LLM-derived semantic information is incorporated into the LAE framework. While using LLMs for recommendation and combining LLM embeddings with collaborative filtering are not entirely new, the two-phase optimization strategy and the semantic-guided regularization are a distinctive contribution. Constructing the semantic correlation matrix *S* before learning the user-item interaction matrix *B*, and using *S* as a regularizer is a clever way of integrating heterogeneous information.  The closed-form solutions for both phases are also a significant advantage in terms of computational efficiency and optimality. However, the novelty is somewhat incremental.  Previous works have already explored different ways of incorporating textual information (even LLM-derived) into recommendation models. The innovation resides in *how* it is achieved within the *specific* LAE framework.

**Significance:** The paper's significance stems from several factors:

*   **Performance Improvement:** The reported performance gains over state-of-the-art baselines (especially on long-tail items) are substantial, suggesting a practical improvement for real-world recommendation systems. The gains reported over pure-interaction LAEs, Tag based LAEs, and other LLM integrated methods are all strong points that add to significance.
*   **Computational Efficiency:**  Closed-form solutions make L³AE computationally efficient, which is a major advantage for large-scale recommendation tasks. This makes the method more appealing for deployment in resource-constrained environments.
*   **Generalizability:** The method is demonstrated on three different datasets, showing some generalizability across different product categories.
*   **Interpretability:** LAEs, being linear models, are inherently more interpretable than deep neural networks. This can be an advantage for understanding the model's behavior and building trust.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Exploration of LLMs:** The paper uses a relatively standard prompting method to extract item embeddings from LLMs. It doesn't explore more sophisticated techniques for leveraging LLMs' generative capabilities or using them to refine item descriptions. It is unclear how sensitive the performance is to the choice of LLM backbone or the prompting strategy. A more thorough ablation study on these aspects would strengthen the paper.
*   **Lack of Deeper Analysis:** While performance metrics are reported, the paper lacks a deeper analysis of *why* L³AE performs better. What specific types of items benefit most from the semantic regularization? Does it improve recommendations for cold-start users or items? Deeper insights into the model's behavior would make the paper more impactful. Specifically a discussion on the semantic information in the LLM vs. the interaction data and how the regularization influences both would be welcome.
*   **Reliance on Closed-Form Solutions:** While closed-form solutions are a strength, they also limit the flexibility of the model. It might be difficult to extend L³AE to incorporate other types of side information or to use non-linear transformations.
*   **Incremental Evaluation of AlphaRec:** There's a significant amount of incremental evaluation as to how L3AE is better than AlphaRec and other methods which are very similar. It would be more useful to see how it integrates with other models, or why it is so successful in long-tail situations, with additional insights.

**Score and Justification:**

I assign a score of **7.5/10**.

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and well-engineered approach (L³AE) to integrating LLMs into the LAE framework for recommendation systems. The two-phase optimization strategy with semantic-guided regularization is a valuable contribution, and the closed-form solutions ensure computational efficiency. The experimental results demonstrate significant performance improvements over state-of-the-art baselines, especially on long-tail items. The major strengths of the paper are the significant performance improvement with respect to similar methods, and the computational efficiency gained via close-form solutions.

However, the paper also has some limitations. The exploration of LLM capabilities is somewhat basic, and the analysis of the model's behavior could be more in-depth. The lack of exploration regarding parameter sensitivity, ablation studies for different phases, and deeper reasoning behind why L3AE works is a detractor. Overall, it is a significant improvement for the state of the art, even if the innovations are very closely related to other methods.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### ProMed: Shapley Information Gain Guided Reinforcement Learning for Proactive Medical LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13514v1)
- **Authors**: Hongxin Ding, Baixiang Huang, Yue Fang, Weibin Liao, Xinke Jiang, Zheng Li, Junfeng Zhao, Yasha Wang
- **Abstract**: Interactive medical questioning is essential in real-world clinical consultations, where physicians must actively gather information from patients. While medical Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown impressive capabilities in static medical question answering, they predominantly operate under a reactive paradigm: generating answers directly without seeking additional information, which risks incorrect diagnoses in such interactive settings. To address this limitation, we propose ProMed, a reinforcement learning (RL) framework that transitions medical LLMs toward a proactive paradigm, equipping them with the ability to ask clinically valuable questions before decision-making. At the core of ProMed is the Shapley Information Gain (SIG) reward, which quantifies the clinical utility of each question by combining the amount of newly acquired information with its contextual importance, estimated via Shapley values. We integrate SIG into a two-stage training pipeline: (1) SIG-Guided Model Initialization uses Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) to construct high-reward interaction trajectories to supervise the model, and (2) SIG-Augmented Policy Optimization, which integrates SIG and enhances RL with a novel SIG-guided Reward Distribution Mechanism that assigns higher rewards to informative questions for targeted optimization. Extensive experiments on two newly curated partial-information medical benchmarks demonstrate that ProMed significantly outperforms state-of-the-art methods by an average of 6.29% and delivers a 54.45% gain over the reactive paradigm, while also generalizing robustly to out-of-domain cases.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the ProMed paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the limitations of medical Large Language Models (LLMs) that operate primarily in a reactive mode, generating answers directly based on initial input without seeking additional information. This can lead to misdiagnosis. The authors propose ProMed, a reinforcement learning (RL) framework that transitions medical LLMs toward a proactive paradigm, enabling them to ask clinically valuable questions before making decisions.  ProMed utilizes a novel Shapley Information Gain (SIG) reward, quantifying the clinical utility of each question by combining the amount of newly acquired information with its contextual importance estimated via Shapley values from cooperative game theory. The framework involves two stages: (1) SIG-Guided Model Initialization, using Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) and SIG to construct high-reward interaction trajectories for supervised warm-up; and (2) SIG-Augmented Policy Optimization, enhancing RL with a SIG-guided Reward Distribution Mechanism that prioritizes informative questions for targeted optimization. Extensive experiments on newly curated partial-information medical benchmarks demonstrate ProMed's superiority over state-of-the-art methods and a significant gain over the reactive paradigm, while also exhibiting robust out-of-domain generalization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the application of reinforcement learning with a cooperative game theory-based reward (Shapley Information Gain - SIG) to guide medical LLMs to ask relevant questions for better diagnosis. While RL for LLMs isn't entirely new, the SIG reward function is a unique contribution. The two-stage training pipeline, combining MCTS-based initialization and SIG-augmented policy optimization, further adds to the novelty. Prior work using prompt engineering or SFT has been shown to be inadequate in creating proactive, adaptable questioning, which underscores ProMed's advancement. Also, the explicit modeling of relationships among facts via shapely values gives an edge over other uncertainty based methods.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from addressing a crucial shortcoming of current medical LLMs: their tendency to operate reactively, which can lead to misdiagnosis in real-world clinical settings. By developing a framework that encourages proactive information-seeking, ProMed has the potential to improve the accuracy and safety of medical LLMs.  The experimental results, particularly the significant performance gains compared to existing methods and the robust out-of-domain generalization, support the practical value of the framework. The curation of publicly available benchmarks further contributes to the field by creating a valuable resource for future research.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The problem definition is well-motivated and relevant to real-world clinical applications.
    *   The SIG reward function is a principled and innovative way to quantify the clinical utility of questions, considering both information gain and contextual importance.
    *   The two-stage training pipeline is well-designed and effectively integrates SIG into the RL process.
    *   The experimental results are comprehensive and demonstrate the effectiveness of ProMed across different models and benchmarks.
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the technical details of the framework.
    *   Public benchmarks facilitate reproducibility and future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The evaluation is primarily conducted in a simulated dialogue environment. This does not entirely capture the complexities of real-world patient interactions. The simulated patient also adds another variable that could impact results.
    *   The paper could benefit from more in-depth analysis and discussion of the limitations and potential biases of the LLMs used in the framework.
    *   The computational cost of using shapely values.
    *   While the paper shows strong quantitative results, additional qualitative analysis showcasing the types of questions ProMed asks in different scenarios would strengthen the claims further.
    *   The reliance on textual medical facts might limit the application to cases where such facts are readily available and structured.

*   **Potential Influence:** ProMed has the potential to significantly influence the development of future medical LLMs by shifting the focus from reactive answering to proactive information-seeking. The SIG reward function and the two-stage training pipeline could serve as a foundation for other researchers to build upon. It might encourage the creation of better medical LLMs.

*   **Justification for Score:**  The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of medical LLMs by addressing a critical limitation of current models. The SIG reward function and the two-stage training pipeline are innovative and effective. The experimental results are compelling. However, the simulated dialogue environment and reliance on text-based data somewhat limit the scope and real-world applicability of the findings. The reliance on LLMs makes the proposed model very complex and computationally heavy.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### 2D Gaussians Meet Visual Tokenizer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13515v1)
- **Authors**: Yiang Shi, Xiaoyang Guo, Wei Yin, Mingkai Jia, Qian Zhang, Xiaolin Hu, Wenyu Liu, Xinggang Wan
- **Abstract**: The image tokenizer is a critical component in AR image generation, as it determines how rich and structured visual content is encoded into compact representations. Existing quantization-based tokenizers such as VQ-GAN primarily focus on appearance features like texture and color, often neglecting geometric structures due to their patch-based design. In this work, we explored how to incorporate more visual information into the tokenizer and proposed a new framework named Visual Gaussian Quantization (VGQ), a novel tokenizer paradigm that explicitly enhances structural modeling by integrating 2D Gaussians into traditional visual codebook quantization frameworks. Our approach addresses the inherent limitations of naive quantization methods such as VQ-GAN, which struggle to model structured visual information due to their patch-based design and emphasis on texture and color. In contrast, VGQ encodes image latents as 2D Gaussian distributions, effectively capturing geometric and spatial structures by directly modeling structure-related parameters such as position, rotation and scale. We further demonstrate that increasing the density of 2D Gaussians within the tokens leads to significant gains in reconstruction fidelity, providing a flexible trade-off between token efficiency and visual richness. On the ImageNet 256x256 benchmark, VGQ achieves strong reconstruction quality with an rFID score of 1.00. Furthermore, by increasing the density of 2D Gaussians within the tokens, VGQ gains a significant boost in reconstruction capability and achieves a state-of-the-art reconstruction rFID score of 0.556 and a PSNR of 24.93, substantially outperforming existing methods. Codes will be released soon.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Visual Gaussian Quantization (VGQ), a novel image tokenizer architecture that incorporates 2D Gaussian Splatting into the traditional vector quantization framework used in tokenizers like VQ-GAN.  VGQ aims to improve the modeling of geometric structures in images, which are often overlooked by patch-based tokenizers focused on texture and color.  The approach uses a dual-branch architecture: one branch based on VQ-GAN and the other encoding image latents as 2D Gaussian distributions.  The Gaussian parameters (position, rotation, scale) are discretized using codebooks. A fused feature map is then decoded to reconstruct the image. They also extend the model to VGQ-multigs, which increases the density of 2D Gaussians within tokens. Experiments on ImageNet demonstrate that VGQ and VGQ-multigs achieve state-of-the-art reconstruction performance, particularly in capturing fine-grained details and geometric structures, especially within text and edge regions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the integration of 2D Gaussian Splatting into an image tokenizer. While 2D Gaussian Splatting has been previously used for image representation and 3D reconstruction, its application *within* the tokenization pipeline is relatively new. The design of the dual-branch architecture and the method for discretizing and fusing Gaussian parameters contribute to the novelty. The authors present a decent attempt at combining two existing technologies, but the approach is relatively straightforward and incremental. The extension to VGQ-multigs, while providing a performance boost, is a fairly predictable extension.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a valid limitation of existing tokenizers, their difficulty in capturing precise geometric structure. Improvements in geometric modeling can benefit various downstream tasks, including image generation, editing, and compression. The empirical results demonstrate a clear improvement over existing methods on the reconstruction task (quantified by rFID, PSNR and SSIM). While the improvement is significant, the ultimate impact depends on how well VGQ performs in *generative* tasks, which is only hinted at in the introduction and not directly demonstrated by the experimental results. The claim of "state-of-the-art" reconstruction performance needs to be balanced by the fact that they are only benchmarked on a relatively narrow task.

* **Strengths:**
    * Clear problem statement and well-defined approach.
    * Sound technical implementation, combining VQ and Gaussian splatting.
    * Strong quantitative results for image reconstruction, demonstrating the effectiveness of the 2D Gaussian representation.
    * Detailed ablations that validate the contribution of each design choice.
    * Useful visualization that helps demonstrate the improvement.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * Limited experimental evaluation. The paper primarily focuses on image *reconstruction*. It lacks comprehensive evaluation on generative tasks where the improved geometric modeling would potentially have a greater impact. Downstream tasks such as image generation or editing are not explicitly tested.
    * Incremental contribution. It does build on prior methods to create a novel architecture, the core idea of combining VQ and gaussian representations, although effective, is not necessarily ground-breaking.
    * The theoretical analysis, while present, is somewhat rudimentary and does not offer deep insight into the benefits of the proposed fusion technique.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in image tokenization by highlighting the importance of geometric modeling and introducing a new way to incorporate structural information into discrete latent representations. However, its impact will depend on its adoption and performance in more diverse and complex downstream tasks.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel approach to image tokenization with demonstrable improvements in image reconstruction. However, the overall contribution is somewhat incremental, and the limited scope of the experimental evaluation makes it difficult to gauge its full potential. The theoretical analysis lacks depth. These limitations suggest that a score of 6 is appropriate.

### Saudi-Dialect-ALLaM: LoRA Fine-Tuning for Dialectal Arabic Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13525v1)
- **Authors**: Hassan Barmandah
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) for Arabic are still dominated by Modern Standard Arabic (MSA), with limited support for Saudi dialects such as Najdi and Hijazi. This underrepresentation hinders their ability to capture authentic dialectal variation. Using a privately curated Saudi Dialect Instruction dataset (Hijazi and Najdi; 5,466 synthetic instruction-response pairs; 50/50 split), we LoRA-tune ALLaM-7B-Instruct-preview, the first foundation model developed in Saudi Arabia, for Saudi dialect generation. We investigate two variants: (i) Dialect-Token training, which prepends an explicit dialect tag to the instruction, and (ii) No-Token training, which omits the tag at formatting time. Evaluation on a held-out test set combines an external dialect classifier with text fidelity metrics (chrF++ and BERTScore) and diversity measures. The Dialect-Token model achieves the best control, raising the Saudi rate from 47.97% to 84.21% and reducing MSA leakage from 32.63% to 6.21%; fidelity also improves (chrF++ +3.53, BERTScore +0.059). Both LoRA variants outperform strong generic instruction models (Falcon-7B-Instruct, Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct, Qwen-2.5-7B-Instruct, AceGPT-v2-8B-Chat, JAIS-13B-Chat) in dialect control and fidelity, while avoiding metadata-tag echoing that these baselines frequently exhibit. We do not release the dataset or any model weights/adapters; instead, we release training/evaluation/inference code and a detailed datasheet (schema and aggregate statistics) to support independent verification.
- **Summary**: The paper presents "Saudi-Dialect-ALLaM," a LoRA fine-tuned version of the ALLaM-7B-Instruct model specifically designed for Saudi dialect generation (Hijazi and Najdi). The authors curated a private synthetic instruction dataset consisting of 5,466 pairs and experimented with two training strategies: Dialect-Token Conditioning and No-Token Conditioning.  Evaluation combines an external dialect classifier (MARBERTv2) with text fidelity metrics (chrF++, BERTScore) and diversity measures. The Dialect-Token model demonstrated superior performance, increasing the Saudi dialect identification rate from 47.97% to 84.21% and reducing MSA leakage from 32.63% to 6.21%. The paper emphasizes reproducibility by releasing the training/evaluation/inference code and a detailed datasheet of the private dataset, while deliberately not releasing the dataset or model weights.

**Novelty and Significance Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a crucial gap in Arabic LLMs: the underrepresentation of Saudi dialects. While Arabic LLMs are improving, dialectal support, especially for Saudi dialects, remains a challenge. The use of a private, balanced Hijazi/Najdi dataset and LoRA fine-tuning of ALLaM-7B-Instruct is a straightforward, but practically valuable approach.

**Strengths:**

*   **Focus on Underrepresented Dialects:** The paper directly tackles the issue of insufficient Saudi dialect support in Arabic LLMs. This is a relevant and practical problem.
*   **Rigorous Evaluation:** The evaluation methodology is comprehensive, combining an external dialect classifier with text fidelity and diversity metrics. The inclusion of a human evaluation also strengthens the validity of the findings.
*   **Emphasis on Reproducibility:** Releasing code and a detailed dataset datasheet allows for independent verification of the results, increasing the trustworthiness of the study.
*   **Comparison of Conditioning Strategies:** The comparison of Dialect-Token vs. No-Token conditioning provides valuable insights into the effectiveness of explicit dialect supervision.
*   **Performance:** The achieved improvements in Saudi dialect fidelity and MSA leakage reduction are significant.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Private Dataset:** The dataset's private nature limits broader impact and independent dataset analysis. While the datasheet mitigates this, it is not the same as having access to the raw data for scrutiny.
*   **Synthetic Data:** The use of synthetic data, while necessary given the limited resources, raises concerns about the model's ability to generalize to real-world Saudi dialect usage. This is acknowledged in the limitations.
*   **Limited Scope:** The focus is exclusively on Hijazi and Najdi dialects. While this is a valuable starting point, other Saudi dialects are left unaddressed.
*   **MARBERTv2 as Proxy:** Using the MARBERTv2's GLF label as a proxy for Saudi dialects introduces potential inaccuracies, as GLF can encompass dialects beyond Saudi Arabia.
*   **Incremental Methodology:** LoRA tuning an existing model with a specific dataset isn't groundbreaking. The core idea is sound and the execution is solid, but the technical contribution isn't profoundly innovative.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field by addressing the underrepresentation of Saudi dialects in Arabic LLMs. The rigorous evaluation and focus on reproducibility are strong points. However, the private, synthetic dataset and incremental methodology limit the overall impact. While the practical improvements are significant, the novelty is somewhat limited. The work offers a strong template for future research but does not represent a paradigm shift. For these reasons, the paper is not ground breaking but still provides solid contributions.

### MATA (māta): Mindful Assessment of the Telugu Abilities of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13526v1)
- **Authors**: Chalamalasetti Kranti, Sowmya Vajjala
- **Abstract**: In this paper, we introduce MATA, a novel evaluation dataset to assess the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) in Telugu language, comprising 729 carefully curated multiple-choice and open-ended questions that span diverse linguistic dimensions. We evaluate 11 open-weight and closed-source LLMs on our dataset and present a fine-grained analysis of their performance. Further, we empirically show how LLMs rely on superficial heuristics such as answer position and distractor patterns for multiple-choice questions. Finally, we also compare LLM-as-a-judge evaluation with human evaluation for open-ended questions and draw some conclusions on its reliability in a low-resource language. We argue that such fine-grained evaluation is essential for understanding model limitations and can inform the development of more linguistically capable LLMs, while also serving as a foundation for future research in Telugu NLP.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MATA, a novel evaluation dataset designed to assess the Telugu language capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs).  MATA consists of 729 curated multiple-choice and open-ended questions covering various linguistic dimensions. The authors evaluate 11 LLMs (both open-weight and closed-source) using MATA and provide a fine-grained analysis of their performance. They also investigate whether LLMs rely on superficial heuristics for multiple-choice questions and compare LLM-as-a-judge evaluation with human evaluations for open-ended questions, highlighting concerns about the LLM's reliability as a judge in low-resource languages.  The authors argue that such focused, granular evaluations are essential for understanding model limitations and driving the development of more linguistically competent LLMs for Telugu NLP.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The creation of a Telugu-specific evaluation dataset (MATA) is a significant contribution, given the relative lack of resources for Telugu NLP. It explicitly addresses the shortcomings of relying on translated datasets or web-scraped data, focusing on grammatically complex, culturally embedded linguistic features. The explicit focus on diverse dimensions of Telugu linguistic competence, including grammar, vocabulary, idioms, and proverbs, moves beyond simple factual knowledge evaluations. *However*, the novelty is somewhat tempered by the fact that similar datasets are increasingly being developed for other low-resource languages. The core concept of a language-specific benchmark isn't entirely new, but the specific content tailored to Telugu is.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in several areas:

    *   **Highlighting LLM Limitations:** The study exposes the limitations of existing LLMs in handling the nuances of a relatively low-resource language like Telugu, even for tasks considered simple for native speakers. The analysis of error patterns and reliance on heuristics is insightful.
    *   **Evaluation Methodology:** The rigorous evaluation methodology, including the comparison between English and Telugu prompts, robustness testing of multiple-choice questions, and the analysis of LLM-as-a-judge reliability, provides valuable insights for future evaluation studies.
    *   **Community Resource:**  The creation of MATA itself provides a valuable resource to the Telugu NLP research community.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Careful Data Curation:** The manual curation of the dataset from educational materials enhances its quality and relevance.
    *   **Fine-Grained Analysis:** The detailed performance analysis across different categories and question types offers a nuanced understanding of LLM strengths and weaknesses.
    *   **Investigation of Evaluation Methods:** The critical examination of LLM-as-a-judge and robustness of multiple-choice questions is important.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dataset Size:** While the manual curation is commendable, a dataset size of 729 questions might be considered relatively small for comprehensively evaluating LLMs, although understandable, it should be improved upon in future iterations of the data set.
    *   **LLM-as-a-Judge Reliability:** The findings on LLM-as-a-judge are concerning. While they highlight the limitations, further investigation into prompt engineering and fine-tuning of the judge LLM could be beneficial. A deeper analysis into the types of questions the models are having a hard time with might be useful.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The investigation is limited to one low-resource language. While the findings are valuable for Telugu NLP, it is not clear how well they generalise to other low-resource languages.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning this paper a score of **7**.

*   The creation of MATA is a valuable and timely contribution to the Telugu NLP community (2 points).
*   The rigorous evaluation methodology and fine-grained analysis reveal significant insights into the strengths and weaknesses of current LLMs and evaluation techniques (3 points).
*   The paper also highlights a potential problem with the use of LLM as a judge with low-resource languages (1 point)
*   However, the limited dataset size, a somewhat incremental degree of novelty in the overall concept (language-specific benchmark), and some open questions regarding LLM-as-a-judge reliability hold the paper back from a higher score (deduction of 3 points).

While similar datasets have been developed for other languages, MATA's specific content, rigorous methodology, and focus on evaluation biases make it a solid contribution to the field, meriting a strong score, even with the identified limitations.
