    *   **Tumor-Aware Strategies:** The tumor-aware loss functions and the explicit use of segmentation masks as conditioning inputs are a significant and practical contribution.  They directly address a key challenge in medical image synthesis: generating realistic lesions.
    *   **Subtraction-Image Prediction:**  The focus on predicting the *difference* image rather than the full post-contrast image is insightful and leads to better performance, likely because it simplifies the learning task. It focuses the model on synthesizing the contrast uptake.
    *   **Reader Study:** The inclusion of a reader study strengthens the paper by providing a clinical perspective on the realism of the generated images, which is crucial for assessing their potential clinical utility.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact of this work is considerable:

    *   **Reducing Contrast Agent Reliance:**  Success in synthesizing DCE-MRI could lead to reduced reliance on contrast agents, mitigating safety concerns, contraindications, and costs.
    *   **Data Augmentation:** The synthesized images could be used for data augmentation to improve the performance of other AI-based diagnostic tools.
    *   **Understanding Contrast Enhancement:**  The modeling process itself may help researchers better understand the complex factors that determine contrast enhancement in breast tissue.
    *   **Clinical application:** The generation of contrast-enhanced images could be used to complement abbreviated MRI protocols.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Comprehensive experimentation and ablation studies.
    *   Use of a large, publicly available dataset.
    *   Evaluation using quantitative metrics *and* expert readers.
    *   Clear articulation of the problem and the proposed solutions.
    *   The open-source code release enhances reproducibility and impact.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Segmentation Masks:**  The best-performing models rely on tumor segmentation masks *during inference*. In many real-world screening scenarios, these masks are *not* available.  While the paper acknowledges this limitation, the practicality of those models is reduced in certain contexts. Models not relying on segmentation at inference are performing substantially worse.
    *   **Limited Temporal Modeling:** The study focuses on synthesizing a single post-contrast image from a pre-contrast image, not the entire temporal sequence. Dynamic aspects of the contrast uptake could be useful in differential diagnosis, potentially limiting clinical applications.
    *   **Generalization to Other Datasets:**  The results are specific to the MAMA-MIA dataset.  It's important to assess how well the models generalize to data from different scanners, protocols, and patient populations. This could be mentioned in the limitation section.
    *   **Artifacts:** The readers detected artifacts during the study, potentially preventing clinical use of the results.

*   **Overall:** The paper is well-written, technically sound, and makes a valuable contribution to the field of medical image synthesis. The extensive experimentation and the inclusion of a reader study are particularly commendable. The dependence of the best-performing models on segmentation masks limits their applicability somewhat, but the other models still show promise. Also, improving results that rely on segmentation masks can be relevant in other tasks, such as treatment planning.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper demonstrates strong technical work and careful evaluation, specifically concerning the problem of lesion fidelity in generating contrast-enhanced breast MRI images. However, the reliance on segmentation masks in the best-performing models and the limited temporal modeling somewhat reduce the overall score. Generalization remains a potential concern. A score of 7 reflects the paper's clear contributions and potential for impact, tempered by the limitations.

- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 7/10

### DegDiT: Controllable Audio Generation with Dynamic Event Graph Guided Diffusion Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13786v1)
- **Authors**: Yisu Liu, Chenxing Li, Wanqian Zhang, Wenfu Wang, Meng Yu, Ruibo Fu, Zheng Lin, Weiping Wang, Dong Yu
- **Abstract**: Controllable text-to-audio generation aims to synthesize audio from textual descriptions while satisfying user-specified constraints, including event types, temporal sequences, and onset and offset timestamps. This enables precise control over both the content and temporal structure of the generated audio. Despite recent progress, existing methods still face inherent trade-offs among accurate temporal localization, open-vocabulary scalability, and practical efficiency. To address these challenges, we propose DegDiT, a novel dynamic event graph-guided diffusion transformer framework for open-vocabulary controllable audio generation. DegDiT encodes the events in the description as structured dynamic graphs. The nodes in each graph are designed to represent three aspects: semantic features, temporal attributes, and inter-event connections. A graph transformer is employed to integrate these nodes and produce contextualized event embeddings that serve as guidance for the diffusion model. To ensure high-quality and diverse training data, we introduce a quality-balanced data selection pipeline that combines hierarchical event annotation with multi-criteria quality scoring, resulting in a curated dataset with semantic diversity. Furthermore, we present consensus preference optimization, facilitating audio generation through consensus among multiple reward signals. Extensive experiments on AudioCondition, DESED, and AudioTime datasets demonstrate that DegDiT achieves state-of-the-art performances across a variety of objective and subjective evaluation metrics.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "DegDiT," a novel framework for controllable text-to-audio generation. It addresses the challenge of generating audio from text descriptions while providing fine-grained control over event types, temporal sequences, and timestamps. DegDiT utilizes dynamic event graphs to encode events, with nodes representing semantic features, temporal attributes, and inter-event connections. A graph transformer integrates these nodes to produce contextualized event embeddings, guiding a diffusion model for audio generation. The framework incorporates a quality-balanced data selection pipeline to create a diverse and high-quality training dataset. Consensus Preference Optimization (CoPO), a reinforcement learning-based method, is used to leverage multi-dimensional reward signals for comprehensive preference modeling.  Experiments demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on various datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper exhibits a good degree of novelty in its approach to controllable text-to-audio generation. Key novel aspects include:

*   **Dynamic Event Graphs:** The use of dynamic event graphs to represent the structure and temporal dependencies of audio events is a relatively novel and effective approach. This allows for a more structured representation of the audio events compared to simply feeding text into the model.
*   **Quality-Balanced Data Selection:** The data selection process, incorporating hierarchical event annotation and multi-criteria scoring, is a significant contribution that addresses the limitations of existing weakly labeled datasets.
*   **Consensus Preference Optimization (CoPO):** The use of reinforcement learning with multi-dimensional reward signals to train the model is a relatively novel way to train a TTA system.
*   **Comprehensive Framework:** The integration of these components into a single, cohesive framework is also novel.

**Significance:**

The significance of this work lies in addressing key limitations of existing text-to-audio systems:

*   **Improved Temporal Control:** The dynamic event graph approach effectively improves control over the timing and sequencing of sound events, a crucial aspect of controllable audio generation.
*   **Open-Vocabulary Scalability:**  The use of a pre-trained language model (FLAN-T5) enables the system to handle a wider range of audio events, addressing the limitations of fixed-vocabulary approaches.
*   **Enhanced Data Quality:**  The curated dataset contributes to better model training and improved generalization.
*   **Objective and Subjective Improvements:** The experimental results demonstrating state-of-the-art performance across various objective and subjective metrics support the effectiveness of DegDiT.

**Strengths:**

*   **Well-Defined Problem:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges in controllable text-to-audio generation.
*   **Novel Approach:** The proposed method integrates innovative techniques such as dynamic event graphs and consensus preference optimization.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of DegDiT across different datasets and evaluation metrics.
*   **Complete Framework:** The approach is a well-engineered complete TTA system, demonstrating that it is effective in practice.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Complexity:** The paper does not sufficiently address the computational complexity of the proposed approach. The graph transformer and diffusion model are likely computationally intensive, and the paper would benefit from discussing the training and inference time compared to baselines.
*   **Dataset Dependence:** While the quality-balanced data selection improves training, the model's performance is still likely dependent on the quality and diversity of the initial dataset. A discussion of the limitations imposed by the available data would be valuable.
*   **Subjective Evaluation Details:** More detail about the Gemini 2.5 Pro evaluation criteria and setup would strengthen the subjective evaluation. Including information about the specific prompts used and the inter-rater agreement in human evaluations would also be beneficial.

**Potential Influence:**

This work has the potential to influence the direction of research in controllable audio generation. The dynamic event graph representation could become a standard approach for encoding audio event information. The CoPO method could also be adopted for training other generative models that require balancing multiple objectives. The framework sets a new benchmark for performance in this area.

**Score:** 8

**Rationale:** DegDiT is a well-designed and thoroughly evaluated framework that introduces several novel contributions to the field of controllable text-to-audio generation. It addresses key limitations of existing methods and achieves state-of-the-art performance. The strengths of the paper outweigh the minor weaknesses. The approach has a high degree of novelty and significance, with strong potential to influence future research in this area. However, the relatively limited discussion about computational complexity, a deeper investigation into data limitations, and additional details regarding subjective evaluations prevent it from achieving a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 8/10

### BetaWeb: Towards a Blockchain-enabled Trustworthy Agentic Web
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13787v1)
- **Authors**: Zihan Guo, Yuanjian Zhou, Chenyi Wang, Linlin You, Minjie Bian, Weinan Zhang
- **Abstract**: The rapid development of large language models (LLMs) has significantly propelled the development of artificial intelligence (AI) agents, which are increasingly evolving into diverse autonomous entities, advancing the LLM-based multi-agent systems (LaMAS). However, current agentic ecosystems remain fragmented and closed. Establishing an interconnected and scalable paradigm for Agentic AI has become a critical prerequisite. Although Agentic Web proposes an open architecture to break the ecosystem barriers, its implementation still faces core challenges such as privacy protection, data management, and value measurement. Existing centralized or semi-centralized paradigms suffer from inherent limitations, making them inadequate for supporting large-scale, heterogeneous, and cross-domain autonomous interactions. To address these challenges, this paper introduces the blockchain-enabled trustworthy Agentic Web (BetaWeb). By leveraging the inherent strengths of blockchain, BetaWeb not only offers a trustworthy and scalable infrastructure for LaMAS but also has the potential to advance the Web paradigm from Web3 (centered on data ownership) towards Web3.5, which emphasizes ownership of agent capabilities and the monetization of intelligence. Beyond a systematic examination of the BetaWeb framework, this paper presents a five-stage evolutionary roadmap, outlining the path of LaMAS from passive execution to advanced collaboration and autonomous governance. We also conduct a comparative analysis of existing products and discuss key challenges of BetaWeb from multiple perspectives. Ultimately, we argue that deep integration between blockchain and LaMAS can lay the foundation for a resilient, trustworthy, and sustainably incentivized digital ecosystem. A summary of the enabling technologies for each stage is available at https://github.com/MatZaharia/BetaWeb.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces BetaWeb, a blockchain-enabled trustworthy Agentic Web, as a solution to the limitations of current fragmented and closed agentic ecosystems. It proposes a five-stage evolutionary roadmap for LaMAS (LLM-based multi-agent systems) development, moving from passive execution to advanced collaboration and autonomous governance. BetaWeb leverages blockchain to provide a trustworthy and scalable infrastructure, addressing challenges related to privacy, data management, and value measurement. It argues that this approach can advance the Web paradigm from Web3 (data ownership) to Web3.5 (agent capability ownership and intelligence monetization).  The paper also analyzes existing products and discusses the key challenges involved in realizing BetaWeb.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The idea of integrating blockchain with LLM agents isn't entirely new, but this paper provides a structured framework and a detailed evolutionary roadmap. The concept of moving from Web3 to Web3.5 focusing on agent capability ownership is novel and presents a compelling vision. The five-stage roadmap, while somewhat intuitive, provides a useful conceptual tool for understanding the development trajectory.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a key challenge in the development of agentic AI: the lack of a trustworthy and scalable infrastructure for collaboration. If realized, BetaWeb could unlock the full potential of LLM agents by enabling seamless, secure, and incentivized interactions. It could also significantly impact how value is created and distributed in the digital economy, moving beyond data ownership towards a capability-based model. The framework's ability to address privacy, data management, and incentivize contributions in decentralized setups are critical for the responsible and sustainable development of agentic AI.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clearly articulates the limitations of existing agentic ecosystems.
    *   Presents a well-defined architecture and a systematic five-stage evolutionary roadmap.
    *   Provides a comprehensive analysis of key challenges and open problems.
    *   Offers a compelling vision of a future Web powered by collaborative and autonomous agents.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper is largely conceptual; it lacks concrete implementation details or experimental results. The github repository link is useful, but likely to evolve over time.
    *   The evolutionary stages, while logical, might seem somewhat idealized and linear. The real-world development path is likely to be more complex and iterative.
    *   The discussion of existing products is brief and could be more comprehensive and critical.
    *   The discussion on social, ethical, and regulatory implications, while present, lacks significant depth and could be further expanded.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence the direction of research in agentic AI, blockchain, and decentralized systems. It provides a valuable conceptual framework for building trustworthy and scalable agentic webs, and could inspire further research on specific technical challenges and governance models. The roadmap provides helpful guidance for developers and researchers in this space.
*   **Score Justification:** The paper presents a compelling vision and a well-structured framework for a blockchain-enabled trustworthy Agentic Web. Its conceptual novelty and the significance of the problem it addresses are noteworthy. However, its lack of concrete implementation details and the somewhat idealized nature of the evolutionary roadmap limit its immediate impact.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.MA
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Fully Transformer Based Multimodal Framework for Explainable Cancer Image Segmentation Using Radiology Reports
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13796v1)
- **Authors**: Enobong Adahada, Isabel Sassoon, Kate Hone, Yongmin Li
- **Abstract**: We introduce Med-CTX, a fully transformer based multimodal framework for explainable breast cancer ultrasound segmentation. We integrate clinical radiology reports to boost both performance and interpretability. Med-CTX achieves exact lesion delineation by using a dual-branch visual encoder that combines ViT and Swin transformers, as well as uncertainty aware fusion. Clinical language structured with BI-RADS semantics is encoded by BioClinicalBERT and combined with visual features utilising cross-modal attention, allowing the model to provide clinically grounded, model generated explanations. Our methodology generates segmentation masks, uncertainty maps, and diagnostic rationales all at once, increasing confidence and transparency in computer assisted diagnosis. On the BUS-BRA dataset, Med-CTX achieves a Dice score of 99% and an IoU of 95%, beating existing baselines U-Net, ViT, and Swin. Clinical text plays a key role in segmentation accuracy and explanation quality, as evidenced by ablation studies that show a -5.4% decline in Dice score and -31% in CIDEr. Med-CTX achieves good multimodal alignment (CLIP score: 85%) and increased confi dence calibration (ECE: 3.2%), setting a new bar for trustworthy, multimodal medical architecture.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Med-CTX, a fully transformer-based multimodal framework for explainable breast cancer ultrasound segmentation. It integrates clinical radiology reports, structured using BI-RADS descriptors, with ultrasound images. Med-CTX employs a dual-branch visual encoder (ViT and Swin Transformer), uncertainty-aware cross-modal attention fusion (using BioClinicalBERT for text), and a multi-task transformer decoder to generate segmentation masks, uncertainty maps, and clinically grounded diagnostic explanations. The model is pre-trained using a CLIP-style contrastive learning approach adapted for the medical domain. Experiments on the BUS-BRA dataset show superior performance compared to existing methods like U-Net, ViT, and Swin Transformer in terms of Dice score and IoU. Ablation studies highlight the importance of clinical text and individual components. The model also demonstrates improved confidence calibration and multimodal alignment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates novelty in several key areas:

    *   **Multimodal Integration with BI-RADS:** Integrating structured BI-RADS descriptors and free-text radiology reports directly into the segmentation pipeline, particularly in an uncertainty-aware manner, is a significant contribution. Most existing segmentation models either ignore this valuable clinical context or treat it as a separate post-processing step.
    *   **Dual-Branch Hybrid Encoder:** The use of both ViT and Swin Transformer within a dual-branch encoder to capture both global and local features addresses a known limitation in existing transformer-based medical image segmentation models. The adaptive fusion gate further refines this approach.
    *   **Explanation Generation:** The joint optimization of segmentation and explanation generation within a single framework, incorporating BI-RADS rules and neural language generation, is a significant advancement towards trustworthy AI in medical imaging. The explicit inclusion of uncertainty in the explanation process is particularly commendable.
    *   **Contrastive pretraining adaptation:** The adaption of contrastive pretraining methods such as CLIP for use with medical ultrasound images.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical challenge in medical AI: the "trust gap." By providing explainable decisions, actionable uncertainty estimates, and alignment with domain standards (BI-RADS), Med-CTX aims to improve clinical adoption and decision-making. The high segmentation accuracy and the qualitative results showcasing clinically relevant explanations are promising.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Approach:** Med-CTX offers a complete solution, addressing segmentation, explanation, and uncertainty quantification within a single framework.
    *   **Strong Performance:** The empirical results demonstrate significant improvements over existing baselines.
    *   **Well-Designed Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each component.
    *   **Clear and Well-Written Paper:** The paper is well-structured and clearly explains the methodology.
    *   **Uses a standardized dataset:** The use of the BUS-BRA dataset provides a useful baseline for other models.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dataset Limitation:** The study is limited to a single dataset (BUS-BRA). While BUS-BRA is a good dataset, demonstrating generalizability across different ultrasound datasets (perhaps from different vendors or with varying image quality) would strengthen the findings.
    *   **Computational Complexity:** The use of dual-branch transformers may result in high computational costs. Evaluating the computational efficiency of Med-CTX would be valuable.
    *   **Limited Clinical Validation:** While the paper emphasizes clinical alignment, it lacks direct clinical validation. A study involving radiologists using Med-CTX in a real-world setting would significantly strengthen the impact.
    *   **Explanation Evaluation:** The explanation evaluation relies on BLEU, CIDEr, and METEOR scores, which are primarily designed for general language tasks. They don't necessarily capture the nuanced clinical relevance or accuracy of the generated explanations. A clinician-based evaluation of explanation quality would be stronger.
    *   **Comparison to other medical image generation methods:** The paper makes mention of using CLIP-style contrastive pretraining, which is used by methods such as MedCLIP. This paper could be improved by comparing to the performance of medical image generation methods.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of medical image analysis, particularly in breast cancer diagnosis. The emphasis on explainability and clinical alignment is crucial for building trust in AI systems and facilitating their adoption in clinical practice. The modular design of Med-CTX allows for future extensions, such as incorporating 3D ultrasound data or other imaging modalities.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:** Med-CTX presents a significant advancement in multimodal medical image segmentation by addressing the critical need for explainability and clinical alignment. The integration of structured and unstructured clinical data, the novel dual-branch encoder, and the uncertainty-aware explanation generation mechanism are all noteworthy contributions. The strong performance on the BUS-BRA dataset is encouraging. However, the limitations in generalizability (single dataset), lack of detailed computation efficiency analysis, limited clinical validation, and the absence of nuanced clinical explanation evaluation prevent a higher score. Further work addressing these limitations would greatly enhance the paper's impact and credibility.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Sketch3DVE: Sketch-based 3D-Aware Scene Video Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13797v1)
- **Authors**: Feng-Lin Liu, Shi-Yang Li, Yan-Pei Cao, Hongbo Fu, Lin Gao
- **Abstract**: Recent video editing methods achieve attractive results in style transfer or appearance modification. However, editing the structural content of 3D scenes in videos remains challenging, particularly when dealing with significant viewpoint changes, such as large camera rotations or zooms. Key challenges include generating novel view content that remains consistent with the original video, preserving unedited regions, and translating sparse 2D inputs into realistic 3D video outputs. To address these issues, we propose Sketch3DVE, a sketch-based 3D-aware video editing method to enable detailed local manipulation of videos with significant viewpoint changes. To solve the challenge posed by sparse inputs, we employ image editing methods to generate edited results for the first frame, which are then propagated to the remaining frames of the video. We utilize sketching as an interaction tool for precise geometry control, while other mask-based image editing methods are also supported. To handle viewpoint changes, we perform a detailed analysis and manipulation of the 3D information in the video. Specifically, we utilize a dense stereo method to estimate a point cloud and the camera parameters of the input video. We then propose a point cloud editing approach that uses depth maps to represent the 3D geometry of newly edited components, aligning them effectively with the original 3D scene. To seamlessly merge the newly edited content with the original video while preserving the features of unedited regions, we introduce a 3D-aware mask propagation strategy and employ a video diffusion model to produce realistic edited videos. Extensive experiments demonstrate the superiority of Sketch3DVE in video editing. Homepage and code: http://http://geometrylearning.com/Sketch3DVE/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Sketch3DVE: Sketch-based 3D-Aware Scene Video Editing":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Sketch3DVE, a novel sketch-based method for editing scene videos, particularly those with significant viewpoint changes. It bridges the gap between 2D sketch inputs and 3D video outputs by performing 3D analysis and manipulation. The method first edits the initial frame using a sketch-based image editing technique. Then, it estimates a 3D point cloud and camera parameters from the input video. A point cloud editing approach, using depth maps for alignment, ensures that edited regions are consistent with the original scene's geometry.  A 3D-aware mask propagation strategy preserves unedited regions across frames. Finally, a video diffusion model synthesizes realistic edited videos. The paper demonstrates the method's capabilities in object insertion, removal, and modification, showcasing superior results compared to existing approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a well-integrated system, combining several existing techniques in a novel way. The core innovation lies in its explicit 3D reasoning for video editing based on sketches. Using point clouds and depth maps to align edits within a video with significant viewpoint changes is a crucial contribution. While individual components (sketch-based image editing, point cloud estimation, diffusion models) are not new, their synergistic combination for this specific task demonstrates novelty. However, a number of existing works already use diffusion models for video editing, making this less innovative.
*   **Significance:** Video editing with explicit 3D awareness is a significant challenge, especially when dealing with viewpoint changes. Sketch3DVE addresses this problem effectively, enabling detailed local manipulation in a way that previous methods struggle with. This makes the work valuable for various applications, including content creation, virtual reality, and augmented reality. The paper improves upon previous approaches.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Effective 3D-aware editing: The method effectively handles viewpoint changes, generating realistic edited videos where new content is coherent with the original scene.
    *   Seamless integration: The seamless integration of image editing, 3D analysis, and video diffusion results in high-quality edits.
    *   Clear methodology: The paper clearly explains each step of the proposed method, making it relatively easy to understand and potentially reproduce.
    *   Extensive experimental validation: The paper includes comprehensive qualitative and quantitative results, demonstrating the method's superiority over existing approaches and the importance of its various components.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Reliance on DUSt3R: The method depends on the accuracy of DUSt3R for point cloud and camera parameter estimation. As the authors acknowledge, this reliance can lead to artifacts if DUSt3R performs poorly, especially in challenging scenarios. Furthermore DUSt3R is also diffusion-model based, so the entire system relies on these models.
    *   Limited to Static Scenes:  The method is restricted to mostly static 3D scenes, which means the method isn't applicable to videos with dynamic objects.
    *   Complexity:  The system is complex and relies on several different components, potentially increasing computational cost and limiting real-time applicability.
    *   Limited motion handling:  The supplementary material reveals limitations with 360-degree camera rotations.
    *   Incremental novelty: While the combination of techniques is novel, the underlying methods are well-established, somewhat decreasing the overall contribution.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in video editing by highlighting the importance of explicit 3D reasoning. It can inspire new methods that improve 3D estimation, refine mask propagation, and enhance the integration of diffusion models for video editing. The technique could be further developed with better motion handling, increased robustness for bad camera estimations, or improved computational cost, allowing use in mobile applications.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** The paper presents a well-executed and significant contribution to the field of video editing. While it combines existing techniques, the novel combination and the explicit 3D reasoning enable capabilities that existing methods lack. The extensive experiments and clear presentation of the methodology further strengthen the paper's impact. However, the method's reliance on DUSt3R for 3D estimation, limitations to static scenes, complexity, and incremental novelty slightly temper its overall significance. The score of 7 reflects the contribution's notable advance in video editing capabilities while also recognizing its limitations and room for further development.

- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Communication-Efficient Federated Learning with Adaptive Number of Participants
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13803v1)
- **Authors**: Sergey Skorik, Vladislav Dorofeev, Gleb Molodtsov, Aram Avetisyan, Dmitry Bylinkin, Daniil Medyakov, Aleksandr Beznosikov
- **Abstract**: Rapid scaling of deep learning models has enabled performance gains across domains, yet it introduced several challenges. Federated Learning (FL) has emerged as a promising framework to address these concerns by enabling decentralized training. Nevertheless, communication efficiency remains a key bottleneck in FL, particularly under heterogeneous and dynamic client participation. Existing methods, such as FedAvg and FedProx, or other approaches, including client selection strategies, attempt to mitigate communication costs. However, the problem of choosing the number of clients in a training round remains extremely underexplored. We introduce Intelligent Selection of Participants (ISP), an adaptive mechanism that dynamically determines the optimal number of clients per round to enhance communication efficiency without compromising model accuracy. We validate the effectiveness of ISP across diverse setups, including vision transformers, real-world ECG classification, and training with gradient compression. Our results show consistent communication savings of up to 30\% without losing the final quality. Applying ISP to different real-world ECG classification setups highlighted the selection of the number of clients as a separate task of federated learning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of communication inefficiency in Federated Learning (FL) caused by selecting a fixed number of clients per round. The authors propose Intelligent Selection of Participants (ISP), an adaptive mechanism that dynamically determines the optimal number of clients per round to balance communication costs and model accuracy. ISP formulates the client count selection as an optimization problem and uses a full-client intermediate communication round to estimate the impact of different client subsets. The paper presents experiments on diverse benchmarks (CIFAR-10, Tiny-ImageNet, and ECG data) and shows that ISP can achieve significant communication savings (up to 30%) without compromising model accuracy. The work also demonstrates ISP's versatility by combining it with other communication-efficient techniques, such as gradient compression.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in addressing the underexplored problem of dynamically adapting the *number* of participating clients in FL rounds, rather than just *which* clients to select. While existing methods primarily focus on client selection based on various criteria, this work highlights the importance of determining the optimal participant count to improve communication efficiency. The formulation of the client count selection as an optimization problem and the proposed ISP algorithm are also novel contributions.

*   **Significance:** Communication efficiency is a crucial bottleneck in FL, especially in heterogeneous and dynamic environments. The proposed ISP method offers a practical solution to reduce communication costs without sacrificing model accuracy. The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of ISP across diverse benchmarks, highlighting its potential impact on real-world FL deployments. The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of different components of the ISP algorithm.
    * **Limitations**
    * Requires communication with all clients in intermediate round. It tries to mitigate that using approximation but the trade-off needs to be quantified.
    * The proposed approach involves using all clients in the intermediate communication round to get updates, which could be costly in terms of computation and communication.
    * Implementation complexity is high, need to handle all clients.
    * The analysis assumes each client has similar bandwidth, which may not be the case in a real-world setup.
    * Code not available.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper addresses an important and timely problem in FL.
    *   The proposed ISP method is versatile and can be combined with other communication-efficient techniques.
    *   The experimental results are comprehensive and demonstrate the effectiveness of ISP across diverse benchmarks.
    *   The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of different components of the ISP algorithm.
    *   Well-written and clearly presents the problem, approach, and results.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on a full-client intermediate communication round could be a potential bottleneck in resource-constrained environments. While the paper mentions techniques to mitigate this (e.g., partial participation), a more detailed analysis of the trade-offs between approximation accuracy and computational cost would be beneficial.
    * The complexity of the algorithm is high, requiring good programming ability.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    *   The paper is likely to stimulate further research on adaptive client participation strategies in FL.
    *   The proposed ISP method could be adopted by practitioners to improve the communication efficiency of their FL deployments.
    *   The optimization framework presented in the paper could be extended to address other related problems in FL, such as adaptive resource allocation and privacy-preserving mechanisms.
    *   The paper could influence the design of future FL frameworks and systems by highlighting the importance of dynamic client participation.

*   **Rigour:** The paper is clearly written, well-organized, and provides a rigorous approach to solving the stated problems. The experiments are comprehensive and provides strong validation of the work.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:** The paper makes a novel and significant contribution to the field of Federated Learning by addressing the problem of dynamic client participation. The proposed ISP method offers a practical solution to reduce communication costs without compromising model accuracy. However, the reliance on full-client intermediate communication is a potential bottleneck, and further analysis of the trade-offs between approximation accuracy and computational cost is needed. While practical, the algorithm is complicated, may only be implemented by skilled programmers.
The work can potentially inspire a new direction of research within Federated Learning and contribute to the development of more efficient and scalable FL systems. Thus, the assigned score reflects the paper's novelty, significance, and potential influence on the field.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Beyond Human Judgment: A Bayesian Evaluation of LLMs' Moral Values Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13804v1)
- **Authors**: Maciej Skorski, Alina Landowska
- **Abstract**: How do large language models understand moral dimensions compared to humans? This first large-scale Bayesian evaluation of market-leading language models provides the answer. In contrast to prior work using deterministic ground truth (majority or inclusion rules), we model annotator disagreements to capture both aleatoric uncertainty (inherent human disagreement) and epistemic uncertainty (model domain sensitivity). We evaluate top language models (Claude Sonnet 4, DeepSeek-V3, Llama 4 Maverick) across 250K+ annotations from ~700 annotators on 100K+ texts spanning social media, news, and forums. Our GPU-optimized Bayesian framework processed 1M+ model queries, revealing that AI models typically rank among the top 25\% of human annotators, achieving much better-than-average balanced accuracy. Importantly, we find that AI produces far fewer false negatives than humans, highlighting their more sensitive moral detection capabilities.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Beyond Human Judgment: A Bayesian Evaluation of LLMs' Moral Values Understanding" presents a large-scale evaluation of large language models (LLMs) regarding their understanding of moral values based on Moral Foundations Theory (MFT). The authors introduce a Bayesian framework to model annotator disagreements, capturing both aleatoric and epistemic uncertainty. They evaluated state-of-the-art LLMs (Claude Sonnet 4, DeepSeek-V3, Llama 4 Maverick) on over 250K annotations from diverse sources (social media, news, forums). The results suggest that AI models consistently outperform human annotators in moral foundation detection, achieving better balanced accuracy and significantly reducing false negatives compared to humans. The paper also presents a novel GPU-optimized implementation of Bayesian labels.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Large-Scale Evaluation:** The use of over 250K annotations across diverse sources provides a robust and comprehensive evaluation. This is a significant improvement over smaller-scale studies with limited data.
*   **Bayesian Modeling of Annotator Disagreement:** This is a key methodological strength. Modeling annotator disagreement as uncertainty, rather than assuming a deterministic ground truth, is more realistic and allows for a nuanced analysis of moral judgments. It addresses a significant limitation of prior work.
*   **Emphasis on Statistical Significance/Robustness:** The work explicitly addresses Type I/II error and aims for reproducible results, adding credibility to the findings. The repeated experiments and use of metrics beyond just accuracy (precision, recall, FPR, FNR) is also commendable.
*   **GPU-Optimized Implementation:** The development of a GPU-optimized TensorFlow framework for Bayesian inference is a valuable technical contribution.
*   **Finding on AI Superiority (reduced false negatives):** The finding that AI models produce fewer false negatives is significant and goes against the common narrative that AI systems underpredict moral values. This warrants further exploration.
*   **Clear Presentation of Results:** The paper effectively uses tables and figures to present the results, facilitating easy comparison of model performances across different datasets and moral foundations.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Prompt Engineering Details:** The prompt selection details could be further expanded. While Appendix A offers some explanation, a more thorough justification of the prompt is beneficial, particularly for prompting nuances.
*   **Fixed Effects Modeling Limitations:** Lack of richer demographic metadata in the datasets limits the power to understand subgroup behaviors.
*   **Content Moderation Limitations:** The potential introduction of bias by content moderation filters within AI models is acknowledged but not thoroughly addressed empirically.  A more detailed analysis of the types of content flagged and their potential impact on the results could strengthen the paper.
*   **Generalizability to Other Moral Frameworks:** The evaluation is limited to Moral Foundations Theory (MFT). How these findings translate to other moral frameworks isn't discussed. It is important to remember this evaluation is framework-specific.
*   **Data Availability Limitations:** The authors are clear about the constraints, but it is worth calling out that the data is not perfectly contemporaneous.
*   **Case Study could be expanded:** While the case study provides useful evidence to illustrate the AI's enhanced sensitivity, it could be expanded further to be more persuasive.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper presents a novel approach to evaluating LLMs on moral reasoning by incorporating Bayesian modeling of annotator disagreement. The large-scale evaluation and the finding of AI superiority in reducing false negatives are significant contributions to the field. The work advances the understanding of how LLMs perceive and process moral information, providing valuable insights for developing ethically-aligned AI systems. It moves the field beyond simple accuracy metrics and towards a more nuanced understanding of error profiles.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper is well-executed, rigorously tested and provides valuable insights regarding AI sensitivity to moral context. The use of Bayesian methods to improve ground truth definition is also very valuable.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Prompt-Based One-Shot Exact Length-Controlled Generation with LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13805v1)
- **Authors**: Juncheng Xie, Hung-yi Lee
- **Abstract**: Controlling the length of text produced by large language models (LLMs) remains challenging: models frequently overshoot or undershoot explicit length instructions because they cannot reliably keep an internal token count. We present a prompt-based, one-shot strategy that compels an off-the-shelf LLM to generate exactly a desired number of tokens - words (English) or characters (Chinese) - without any fine-tuning or iterative sampling. The prompt appends countdown markers and explicit counting rules so that the model "writes while counting." We evaluate on four settings: open-ended generation (1-1000 tokens), XSUM summarization, MT-Bench-LI instruction following, and the LIFEBENCH equal-length track. On MT-Bench-LI, strict length compliance with GPT-4.1 leaps from below 30% under naive prompts to above 95% with our countdown prompt, surpassing the popular draft-then-revise baseline, while judged answer quality is preserved. These results show that precise length control can be achieved through prompt engineering alone, offering a lightweight alternative to training- or decoding-based methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CAPEL (Countdown-Aided Prompting for Exact Length), a novel prompt-engineering technique that enables Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate text with precise length control (word or character counts) without requiring fine-tuning or specialized decoding methods. CAPEL appends a countdown marker suffix to standard prompts, instructing the LLM to "write while counting" using explicit counting rules. The method is evaluated across various tasks, including open-ended generation, summarization, instruction following, and a dedicated length-control benchmark. The results demonstrate significant improvements in length compliance compared to naive prompting, often exceeding 95% accuracy, while preserving content quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using countdown markers as a prompt engineering technique for precise length control is relatively novel.  Existing length control methods primarily rely on training or complex decoding algorithms.  CAPEL offers a lightweight, prompt-based alternative. However, the paper is not the first to suggest that LLMs struggle with internal counting, and it builds on the general principle of externalizing computation through prompting (similar to chain-of-thought). The crucial contribution is the specific design of the countdown-marker prompt and its empirical validation.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a practically important problem: the lack of reliable length control in LLMs, especially in scenarios with strict length constraints. The results are compelling, showing significant improvements in length compliance across a range of models and tasks. The method's ease of implementation (no training or model modification required) makes it highly accessible and applicable to both proprietary and open-source LLMs. The ablation studies offer insights into why the technique works, highlighting the counting limitations of transformers and the benefits of externalizing computation. The paper also provides detailed error analysis that identifies potential weaknesses in the method (e.g., sensitivity to safety filters in some smaller models).

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Simplicity and accessibility:** CAPEL is straightforward to implement and requires no model training, fine-tuning, or specialized decoding.
    *   **Strong empirical results:** The paper presents extensive experimental results on diverse tasks and models, demonstrating the effectiveness of CAPEL across various settings.
    *   **Detailed analysis:** The paper provides insightful analysis of the results, including ablation studies, error analysis, and comparisons to baseline methods.
    *   **Practical impact:** CAPEL has the potential to improve the usability of LLMs in applications where precise length control is crucial (e.g., headline generation, constrained creative writing, social media posts).

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Potential issues with smaller models:** The method appears to be less reliable with certain smaller or highly safety-hardened models due to misinterpretations of the countdown markers.
    *   **Limited exploration of prompt variations:** While the paper presents a well-designed prompt, there could be further exploration of different prompt formulations and their impact on performance.
    *   **Dependency on tokenization:** The method's effectiveness may be influenced by the specific tokenization scheme used by the LLM. This influence wasn't fully explored.
    *   **Evaluation Metric limitations**: Exact match is a fairly strict metric, and while the authors analyze the mean absolute error, it does not capture whether the over or undershoot errors are meaningful from the perspective of the application.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper can influence the field by promoting prompt engineering as a viable alternative to more complex methods for length control. It could inspire further research on prompt-based techniques for controlling other aspects of LLM generation.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and effective prompt engineering technique for a practically important problem. The simplicity and accessibility of CAPEL, combined with strong empirical results, make it a valuable contribution to the field. While there are some weaknesses, such as potential issues with certain smaller models and limited exploration of prompt variations, the overall strengths of the paper outweigh its limitations. The paper's focus on ease-of-implementation and strong performance has the potential to impact both researchers and practitioners working with LLMs.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Energy Management and Wake-up for IoT Networks Powered by Energy Harvesting
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13825v1)
- **Authors**: David Ernesto Ruiz-Guirola, Samuel Montejo-Sanchez, Israel Leyva-Mayorga, Zhu Han, Petar Popovski, Onel L. A. Lopez
- **Abstract**: The rapid growth of the Internet of Things (IoT) presents sustainability challenges such as increased maintenance requirements and overall higher energy consumption. This motivates self-sustainable IoT ecosystems based on Energy Harvesting (EH). This paper treats IoT deployments in which IoT devices (IoTDs) rely solely on EH to sense and transmit information about events/alarms to a base station (BS). The objective is to effectively manage the duty cycling of the IoTDs to prolong battery life and maximize the relevant data sent to the BS. The BS can also wake up specific IoTDs if extra information about an event is needed upon initial detection. We propose a K-nearest neighbors (KNN)-based duty cycling management to optimize energy efficiency and detection accuracy by considering spatial correlations among IoTDs' activity and their EH process. We evaluate machine learning approaches, including reinforcement learning (RL) and decision transformers (DT), to maximize information captured from events while managing energy consumption. Significant improvements over the state-ofthe-art approaches are obtained in terms of energy saving by all three proposals, KNN, RL, and DT. Moreover, the RL-based solution approaches the performance of a genie-aided benchmark as the number of IoTDs increases.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenges of sustainability in IoT networks, particularly focusing on energy harvesting (EH) powered deployments for event detection and alarm systems. It proposes a duty-cycling and wake-up management framework, controlled by a base station (BS), to optimize energy efficiency and maximize information gathering from IoT devices (IoTDs). The core of the approach involves: 1) a K-nearest neighbors (KNN)-based duty cycling management to leverage spatial correlations among IoTDs' activity and EH, 2) reinforcement learning (RL), and 3) decision transformers (DT) to dynamically adapt IoTD operation modes. The paper compares these solutions against a random duty cycling benchmark and a genie-aided benchmark (representing an upper bound). The simulation results demonstrate improvements in energy saving and information capture, particularly with the RL-based solution approaching the genie-aided benchmark under high IoTD density.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Problem Relevance:** The paper tackles a crucial problem in IoT – achieving sustainable and energy-efficient operation in self-powered networks.  The shift towards energy harvesting is a recognized trend, making this work timely.
    *   **Multi-faceted Approach:**  The paper explores multiple techniques (KNN, RL, DT), providing a broader perspective and comparison of methods for duty cycling optimization.
    *   **Performance Gains:** The simulation results indicate significant improvements over a basic random duty cycling strategy, which is important. The RL solution demonstrating performance close to a genie-aided benchmark is a positive outcome.
    *   **Well-Defined System Model:** The system model is clearly defined, including models for energy harvesting, power consumption, event sensing, and IoTD state transitions. This allows for a clear understanding of the framework and simulations.
    *   **Addresses Key Trade-offs:** The paper explicitly addresses the key trade-offs between energy saving, information gathering, and misdetection probability, which is important in practical deployments.
    *   **Building on Prior Work:**  The paper clearly builds on the authors' previous work [23], expanding the scope to address a broader optimization goal, from minimal information to maximum information extracted, and considering EH fluctuations.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Novelty Limitations:** While the application of KNN for duty cycling and the comparison against RL and DT is valuable, the individual techniques themselves are not entirely novel.  The key novelty lies in their combination and application within this specific IoT energy harvesting context.
    *   **Complexity of RL Implementation:** The RL-based implementation is complex and likely requires significant computational resources at the BS, which could be a limiting factor in resource-constrained scenarios. While the paper mentions that this learning has a "one-time energy cost," the sustained computational requirements of RL (especially with high IoT density) should be addressed further. This can be explained in the discussion session.
    *   **Genie-Aided Benchmark Practicality:** The genie-aided benchmark, while providing a theoretical upper bound, is fundamentally impractical because it relies on knowing the event epicenter *a priori*. Its value is limited to a theoretical reference.
    *   **Specific Nature of Simulations:** The simulations are conducted with specific parameter settings and a particular spatial distribution of IoTDs. The generalizability of these results to different scenarios (e.g., different EH profiles, irregular deployments, event patterns) needs further exploration.
    *   **Real-World Considerations:** The paper could benefit from a more in-depth discussion of real-world challenges such as channel contention, packet collisions, and the overhead of WuR signaling. While the paper alludes to synchronization updates, a clearer explanation of the channel access and medium arbitration is needed.
    *   **Limited Ablation Studies:** While different weight factors are considered with RL, there aren't detailed ablation studies to explore the specific impact of design choices on individual parameters like the learning rate, the size of mini-batches, or number of neurons of the LSTM and DT transformer, particularly regarding convergence properties and sensitivity.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper contributes to the growing body of research on sustainable IoT and energy-efficient networking. It provides a comparative analysis of different duty-cycling and wake-up management strategies, offering insights into the trade-offs between energy consumption and information capture.
    *   The study of spatial correlations and the integration of machine learning techniques represent a step forward in optimizing resource allocation in EH-powered IoT networks.
    *   The use of DT in this context is a promising area of research, and the paper's findings can inspire further exploration of transformer-based solutions for IoT resource management.
    *   The comparative study provides insights into the relative performance of different methods, useful for guiding practical implementations.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses, the paper represents a solid contribution to the field but has some limitations in novelty and real-world applicability.

*   The problem addressed is highly relevant, and the paper provides a comprehensive comparative analysis of different strategies to duty cycling optimization.
*   The performance gains shown in simulations are promising, particularly the RL-based performance close to a theoretical optimum.
*   However, the reliance on existing techniques for duty cycling and potential complexity limits lower the novelty score somewhat.  The simulations are limited to particular spatial distribution that is not realistic. There aren't detailed ablation studies that explores some specific parameters.
*   The value of the Genie-Aided baseline is limited as it relies on knowing the event epicenter *a priori*, that it is unrealistic in the real world.
*   The practical complexity of implementing and managing the BS-controlled RL algorithm in large-scale deployments needs to be carefully considered.

Therefore, a score of 7 is assigned.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: eess.SY
- **Score**: 7/10

### Latent Interpolation Learning Using Diffusion Models for Cardiac Volume Reconstruction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13826v1)
- **Authors**: Niklas Bubeck, Suprosanna Shit, Chen Chen, Can Zhao, Pengfei Guo, Dong Yang, Georg Zitzlsberger, Daguang Xu, Bernhard Kainz, Daniel Rueckert, Jiazhen Pan
- **Abstract**: Cardiac Magnetic Resonance (CMR) imaging is a critical tool for diagnosing and managing cardiovascular disease, yet its utility is often limited by the sparse acquisition of 2D short-axis slices, resulting in incomplete volumetric information. Accurate 3D reconstruction from these sparse slices is essential for comprehensive cardiac assessment, but existing methods face challenges, including reliance on predefined interpolation schemes (e.g., linear or spherical), computational inefficiency, and dependence on additional semantic inputs such as segmentation labels or motion data. To address these limitations, we propose a novel \textbf{Ca}rdiac \textbf{L}atent \textbf{I}nterpolation \textbf{D}iffusion (CaLID) framework that introduces three key innovations. First, we present a data-driven interpolation scheme based on diffusion models, which can capture complex, non-linear relationships between sparse slices and improves reconstruction accuracy. Second, we design a computationally efficient method that operates in the latent space and speeds up 3D whole-heart upsampling time by a factor of 24, reducing computational overhead compared to previous methods. Third, with only sparse 2D CMR images as input, our method achieves SOTA performance against baseline methods, eliminating the need for auxiliary input such as morphological guidance, thus simplifying workflows. We further extend our method to 2D+T data, enabling the effective modeling of spatiotemporal dynamics and ensuring temporal coherence. Extensive volumetric evaluations and downstream segmentation tasks demonstrate that CaLID achieves superior reconstruction quality and efficiency. By addressing the fundamental limitations of existing approaches, our framework advances the state of the art for spatio and spatiotemporal whole-heart reconstruction, offering a robust and clinically practical solution for cardiovascular imaging.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework called Cardiac Latent Interpolation Diffusion (CaLID) for reconstructing 3D cardiac volumes from sparsely acquired 2D short-axis (SAX) CMR images. CaLID addresses limitations of existing interpolation methods by: 1) Learning a data-driven interpolation scheme using diffusion models in the latent space to capture complex, non-linear relationships between sparse slices; 2) Employing a computationally efficient latent space operation to speed up 3D reconstruction; and 3) Achieving state-of-the-art (SOTA) performance without requiring additional semantic inputs like segmentation labels or motion data.  The framework extends to 2D+T data to effectively model spatiotemporal dynamics and ensure temporal coherence. The authors demonstrate the superior reconstruction quality and efficiency of CaLID through quantitative evaluations and downstream segmentation tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper presents a well-integrated system combining latent diffusion models with a specifically designed training and inference strategy for cardiac MR image reconstruction.
*   **Data-driven interpolation**: Moving away from predefined interpolation schemes and learning directly from data using diffusion models is a strong contribution.  The design and training of the latent diffusion model tailored for this specific task constitutes a genuine novel aspect.
*   **Computational Efficiency**: The significant reduction in computation by operating in the latent space (24x speedup in 3D whole-heart upsampling) is a practical advancement.
*   **Minimal Input Requirements**: Achieving SOTA without requiring extra segmentation labels or motion data is a significant benefit for clinical workflows.  This simplification is a crucial factor in the paper's significance.
*   **Spatiotemporal Extension**: Extending the framework to 2D+T adds substantial value, addressing an essential need in dynamic cardiac imaging.

**Significance:**

The paper's contributions are highly significant due to the potential impact on clinical practice.

*   **Improved Reconstruction Quality**: Higher quality 3D reconstruction from sparse data leads to better diagnostic accuracy and treatment planning.
*   **Increased Efficiency**: The improved speed makes the method more practical for real-world clinical applications.
*   **Simplified Workflow**: Reducing the reliance on additional inputs simplifies the workflow and broadens the applicability of the method.
*   **Clinical Impact**: The paper presents results that suggest that the proposed technique is a viable method of CMR data reconstruction and segmentation. Furthermore, the lack of required ancillary information suggests that it can be easily deployed in a clinical setting.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dependence on the Pre-trained VAE:** The performance depends heavily on the quality of the VAE. If the VAE latent space doesn't capture relevant cardiac features well, the diffusion model will be limited. While the authors train the VAE from scratch, the specific architecture and training hyperparameters may need careful tuning for different datasets.
*   **Limited Ground Truth for Evaluation:** The reliance on qualitative assessment for 3D volume reconstruction is a weakness.  The absence of true 3D data makes it difficult to quantify the accuracy of the reconstructed volumes rigorously. While the long axis visualization provides insight, a direct volumetric comparison would be more compelling.
*   **Comparisons:** The model comparisons in Table 1 are limited, and the implementation details for these models are not provided. Therefore, it is impossible to reproduce the comparison and difficult to verify that these are implemented correctly.
*   **Dataset specific:** It is not necessarily the case that all SAX slices have 8mm spacing, and there is no mention of how the method scales across various image spaces.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant advancement in cardiac MRI reconstruction. The combination of a data-driven latent diffusion model with computational efficiency and minimal input requirements makes it a clinically relevant solution. While the reliance on the VAE and the lack of true 3D ground truth are weaknesses, the strengths outweigh these limitations. The paper offers substantial practical benefits over existing methods.

### SAGA: Learning Signal-Aligned Distributions for Improved Text-to-Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13866v1)
- **Authors**: Paul Grimal, Michaël Soumm, Hervé Le Borgne, Olivier Ferret, Akihiro Sugimoto
- **Abstract**: State-of-the-art text-to-image models produce visually impressive results but often struggle with precise alignment to text prompts, leading to missing critical elements or unintended blending of distinct concepts. We propose a novel approach that learns a high-success-rate distribution conditioned on a target prompt, ensuring that generated images faithfully reflect the corresponding prompts. Our method explicitly models the signal component during the denoising process, offering fine-grained control that mitigates over-optimization and out-of-distribution artifacts. Moreover, our framework is training-free and seamlessly integrates with both existing diffusion and flow matching architectures. It also supports additional conditioning modalities -- such as bounding boxes -- for enhanced spatial alignment. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our approach outperforms current state-of-the-art methods. The code is available at https://github.com/grimalPaul/gsn-factory.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "SAGA: Learning Signal-Aligned Distributions for Improved Text-to-Image Generation" introduces a novel approach to address textual alignment failures in text-to-image (T2I) models.  SAGA learns a high-success-rate distribution conditioned on a target prompt, ensuring that generated images faithfully reflect the corresponding prompts. The method explicitly models the signal component during the denoising process, offering fine-grained control and mitigating out-of-distribution artifacts. It is training-free, integrates seamlessly with existing diffusion and flow matching architectures, and supports additional conditioning modalities like bounding boxes.  The paper presents extensive experimental results demonstrating SAGA's superior performance compared to state-of-the-art methods in terms of textual alignment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the approach of directly learning a distribution over latents conditioned on a text prompt *before* the denoising process, as opposed to manipulating existing latents like GSN or optimizing the initial noise as in InitNO. This shifts the paradigm from point-wise latent adjustment to distribution learning.  The signal-aligned Gaussian approximation and its interpretation from a signal processing perspective are also novel.
*   **Significance:** Textual alignment is a significant challenge in T2I generation, limiting usability and user experience.  SAGA offers a promising solution with several advantages: it's training-free, easy to integrate with existing models, and supports additional conditioning modalities. The performance gains reported in the paper are substantial, particularly on complex prompts with multiple entities.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong Conceptual Foundation:** The paper is well-motivated and clearly explains the limitations of existing approaches. The theoretical justification for the Gaussian approximation and the signal processing interpretation are valuable.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a thorough experimental evaluation with quantitative metrics (TIAM, VQA, CLIP, GenEval) and qualitative results. The ablation studies provide insights into the impact of different components and hyperparameters. The user study further strengthens the findings.
    *   **Practical Applicability:** The method's training-free nature and compatibility with existing models make it readily applicable. The support for bounding box conditioning enhances control over spatial alignment.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:** Like GSN, the method requires backpropagation through the model during inference, which can be computationally expensive.  The paper acknowledges this limitation.
    *   **Dependence on Internal Model Knowledge:**  While the method's reliance on internal knowledge is presented as a strength, it also limits the extent of correction possible. SAGA will never generate something that fundamentally is inconsistent with the base model.
    *   **Hyperparameter Tuning:** The method requires hyperparameter tuning for the optimization process, which can be challenging. While the paper includes a hyperparameter study, the optimal settings may vary depending on the base model and the specific application.
    *   **Variance Learning:** The results for variance learning are somewhat mixed, with limited improvements and even performance degradation in some cases. This suggests that further research is needed to effectively learn and incorporate the covariance structure.

*   **Potential Influence:** SAGA has the potential to influence the field by shifting the focus from point-wise latent manipulation to distribution learning for improved textual alignment. The signal-aligned Gaussian approximation provides a valuable framework for controllable generation. It also opens up avenues for future research, such as exploring alternative forms of control over the learned signal and developing more efficient optimization techniques.

*   **Justification of Score:** The paper presents a novel and well-evaluated approach to a significant problem in T2I generation. The strengths in conceptual foundation, comprehensive evaluation, and practical applicability outweigh the identified weaknesses. While computational cost is a limiting factor, the method's performance gains and the potential for further research make it a valuable contribution.

### Toward Deployable Multi-Robot Collaboration via a Symbolically-Guided Decision Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13877v1)
- **Authors**: Rathnam Vidushika Rasanji, Jin Wei-Kocsis, Jiansong Zhang, Dongming Gan, Ragu Athinarayanan, Paul Asunda
- **Abstract**: Reinforcement learning (RL) has demonstrated great potential in robotic operations. However, its data-intensive nature and reliance on the Markov Decision Process (MDP) assumption limit its practical deployment in real-world scenarios involving complex dynamics and long-term temporal dependencies, such as multi-robot manipulation. Decision Transformers (DTs) have emerged as a promising offline alternative by leveraging causal transformers for sequence modeling in RL tasks. However, their applications to multi-robot manipulations still remain underexplored. To address this gap, we propose a novel framework, Symbolically-Guided Decision Transformer (SGDT), which integrates a neuro-symbolic mechanism with a causal transformer to enable deployable multi-robot collaboration. In the proposed SGDT framework, a neuro-symbolic planner generates a high-level task-oriented plan composed of symbolic subgoals. Guided by these subgoals, a goal-conditioned decision transformer (GCDT) performs low-level sequential decision-making for multi-robot manipulation. This hierarchical architecture enables structured, interpretable, and generalizable decision making in complex multi-robot collaboration tasks. We evaluate the performance of SGDT across a range of task scenarios, including zero-shot and few-shot scenarios. To our knowledge, this is the first work to explore DT-based technology for multi-robot manipulation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework called Symbolically-Guided Decision Transformer (SGDT) for multi-robot collaboration.  SGDT integrates a neuro-symbolic planner with a goal-conditioned decision transformer (GCDT).  The neuro-symbolic planner, based on PDDL and a large language model (LLM), generates high-level task-oriented plans composed of symbolic subgoals.  The GCDT then performs low-level sequential decision-making, guided by these subgoals.  The framework is evaluated on simulated multi-robot tasks (sandwich making and grocery packing) within the RoCoBench environment, demonstrating improvements in task success rates, few-shot adaptation, and zero-shot generalization compared to a non-symbolically guided approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of combining a neuro-symbolic planner with a decision transformer for multi-robot collaboration has some novelty.  While both neuro-symbolic planning and decision transformers have been used in robotics separately, their specific integration in this hierarchical fashion for *multi-robot* settings appears to be a less explored area. The claim that this is the *first* work exploring DT-based technology for multi-robot manipulation is strong, however, the paper does not sufficiently address previous work. The use of an open-source LLM for planning adds an element of practicality and cost-effectiveness, particularly for real-world deployments. The choice of LLaMA3 as an open source LLM provides benefits for reproducibility but also invites limitations in more complex domains, which the paper does not address.

*   **Significance:**  The potential significance lies in addressing the limitations of traditional reinforcement learning (data-intensive and Markov assumption) for complex multi-robot tasks. The hierarchical structure offers interpretability and potentially better generalization to new scenarios, which are crucial for real-world deployment.  The experimental results, while in simulation, support these claims, showing improved success rates with subgoal guidance and demonstrating few-shot adaptation capabilities. However, the simulated environment, while comprehensive, is less complex than real world scenarios, which may impact overall scalability.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear and well-structured presentation of the SGDT framework.
    *   Demonstration of improved performance with subgoal guidance.
    *   Evaluation of few-shot adaptation and zero-shot generalization, which are important for practical applicability.
    *   Use of an open-source LLM for cost-effective planning.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Real-World Validation:**  The evaluation is conducted solely in simulation.  The gap between simulation and real-world performance in robotics can be significant. The paper needs to discuss this limitation extensively.
    *   **Complexity of Tasks:** While the tasks (sandwich making and grocery packing) are reasonably complex, they are still somewhat constrained.  The framework's scalability to more complex and dynamic environments (e.g., unstructured manufacturing settings) is not thoroughly addressed.
    *   **Limited Comparison to other methods:** the paper lacks a thorough comparison to other recent multi-robot task planning algorithms.
    *   **LLM Prompt Engineering:** The paper needs to explain how the prompts are constructed to provide more insights.
    *   **Lack of Ablation Studies:** it would have been valuable to include more ablation studies to assess the contribution of individual components of the SDGT and the performance and efficiency of each component.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper provides a promising approach to tackling multi-robot collaboration with improved interpretability and adaptability. It could encourage further research into neuro-symbolic approaches combined with decision transformers for robotics and inspire development of more robust and deployable multi-robot systems. The approach of using off-the-shelf LLMs for planning tasks is also a promising approach.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** While the integration of a neuro-symbolic planner with decision transformers is a promising direction, the limitations of the evaluation and the lack of comparison make it difficult to ascertain the truly significant contribution of this work. The authors need to strengthen the comparisons. Thus, while the paper does present a novel approach, the limited real-world validation and insufficient discussion of scalability restrict its current impact.
